## 1. Problem, Operational Scope, and Contributions

Training multi-layer causal Transformers without a global backward pass is a core problem in local credit assignment [1, 2, 3, 9]. A foundational methodological distinction separates **backward-free local credit assignment** from **approximate or manual backpropagation**: disabling automatic differentiation (`torch.no_grad()`) or replacing local analytical derivatives with finite-difference probes does not remove the backward phase if current-step downstream error vectors are multiplied by layer weight matrices in reverse order ($\mathbf{e}_{\text{in}} = \mathbf{e}_{\text{out}} W$) or relayed from downstream blocks to upstream blocks within the same training step.

In Chung's **Weight Maximization (WM) with eligibility traces** [1], each unit $i$ in stage $m$ injects a local forward exploration perturbation $\xi_{n, i}^{(m)}(t)$ during the forward pass of batch $t$, records a local pre-synaptic eligibility trace $Z_{i, :}^{(m)}(t)$, and updates at a subsequent step using the column weight-norm change $\sum_{j} W_{j, i}^{(m+1)} \Delta W_{j, i}^{(m+1)}$ of its outgoing receiver synapses $W_{:, i}^{(m+1)}$.

### 1.1 Operational Definition of Delayed Perturbation-Differential Column-Norm Credit and Pipeline Clocks

To adapt delayed WM to multi-token Transformer minibatches where receiver layers are updated by adaptive or spectrally orthogonalized optimizers (`AdamW` or `Pre-Polar Muon` [7, 8]), we state precisely what feedback our algorithm constructs, how its normalization is parameterized in code, and how pipeline clocks are ordered:

1. **Installed Receiver Displacement vs. Perturbation-Differential Plasticity Tensor:** At step $t$, each receiver layer $m+1$ updates its live weights $W^{(m+1)}(t) = W^{(m+1)}(t-1) + \delta W^{(m+1)}(t-1)$ via its optimizer from the clean batch gradient (or delayed WM pseudo-gradient). To evaluate the directional effect of stage $m$'s forward perturbation $\xi^{(m)}(t_{\text{orig}})$ on receiver $m+1$'s synaptic plasticity without confounding from other stages' noise or optimizer state history, our reward routine (`compute_stream_wm_rewards_chunked` in `exp26`; `compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast` in `exp32`–`exp35`, parameterized by `scale_factor`) constructs for each token stream $\mathcal{S}_g$ ($g \in \{1, \dots, G\}$, $|\mathcal{S}_g| = M_g = BT / G$) a **perturbation-differential plasticity tensor**
   $$
   \Delta \tilde{W}_{j, i}^{(m+1, g)}(t_{\text{orig}}; \text{scale\_factor}) := \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} u_{n, j}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{orig}}) \, \Delta y_{n, i}^{(m)}(t_{\text{orig}}), \qquad \Delta y_{n, i}^{(m)} := y_{n, i}^{\text{pert}}(\sigma \xi^{(m)}) - y_{n, i}^{\text{clean}}(0),
   $$
   where $u_{n, :}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{orig}})$ is the receiver's local post-synaptic modulation vector and $\Delta y_{n, :}^{(m)}(t_{\text{orig}})$ is the perturbed-minus-clean forward activation displacement at stage $m$'s output. At reward construction clock $t_{\text{constr}}$, unit $i$ contracts $\Delta \tilde{W}_{:, i}^{(m+1, g)}(t_{\text{orig}}; \text{scale\_factor})$ with its **live outgoing receiver column** $W_{:, i}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}})$:
   $$
   R_i^{(m, g)}(t_{\text{orig}}, t_{\text{constr}}; \text{scale\_factor}) = \sum_{j=1}^{d_{\text{next}}} W_{j, i}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}}) \, \Delta \tilde{W}_{j, i}^{(m+1, g)}(t_{\text{orig}}; \text{scale\_factor}).
   $$
   The parameter update then divides the stream-summed modulation–input product $\sum_{g=1}^G R_i^{(m, g)} Z_{i, :}^{(m, g)}$ by $N = BT$ (`-(mod_flat.t() @ x_flat) / BT`). Thus, $\Delta \tilde{W}^{(m+1, g)}$ is a local perturbation-differential Hebbian plasticity tensor rather than a decomposition of the installed optimizer displacement $\delta W^{(m+1)}$, and reading $W^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}})$ at construction clock $t_{\text{constr}}$ makes the reward directly sensitive to any receiver weight update $\delta W^{(m+1)}(t-1) = W^{(m+1)}(t) - W^{(m+1)}(t-1)$ installed between origin $t_{\text{orig}}$ and construction $t_{\text{constr}}$.
2. **Three-Clock Pipeline Schedules:** For each stage $m \in \{1, 2, 3\}$, we track $(t_{\text{orig}}, t_{\text{constr}}, t_{\text{inst}})$ relative to installation step $t_{\text{inst}} = t$. Across `exp26`–`exp35`, three distinct clock schedules are evaluated:
   - **True Both-Stages Pre-Install (`both_stages_pre_install`, `exp26`–`exp30` and `exp35` Part B2):** `Stage 3`: $(t, t, t)$; `Stage 2`: $(t-1, t-1 \text{ [pre-Stage3-step]}, t)$ (reward constructed at $t-1$ on $W_{\text{lm}}(t-1)$ prior to Stage 3's batch-$t-1$ update, installed at $t$); `Stage 1`: $(t-2, t-1 \text{ [pre-Stage2-step]}, t)$ (reward constructed and cached at $t-1$ on $W_{\text{proj}}(t-1)$ prior to Stage 2's installation of its batch-$t-2$ update, then installed into Stage 1 at $t$).
   - **Stage-2 Pre-Install / Stage-1 Post-Install (`stage2_pre_install`, `run_exp33_partB.py`, `run_exp34.py`, `exp35` Part B1):** `Stage 3`: $(t, t, t)$; `Stage 2`: $(t-1, t-1 \text{ [pre-Stage3-step]}, t)$ (constructed at $t-1$ prior to Stage 3's update); `Stage 1`: $(t-2, t \text{ [post-Stage2-step]}, t)$ (constructed at $t$ on live $W_{\text{proj}}(t)$ after Stage 2 installed its batch-$t-2$ update at the end of $t-1$).
   - **Strict Post-Installation Construction (`strict_post_install`, `exp32`–`exp35`):** `Stage 3`: $(t, t, t)$; `Stage 2`: $(t-1, t \text{ [post-Stage3-step]}, t)$ (constructed at $t$ on $W_{\text{lm}}(t) = W_{\text{lm}}(t-1) + \delta W_{\text{lm}}(t-1)$ strictly *after* Stage 3 installed its batch-$t-1$ update); `Stage 1`: $(t-2, t \text{ [post-Stage2-step]}, t)$ (constructed at $t$ on $W_{\text{proj}}(t)$ strictly *after* Stage 2 installed its batch-$t-2$ update).
   *(Note: The `CausalAuditLedger` fields in our JSON logs record declared trainer configuration metadata summarizing the code path of each arm rather than runtime hardware profiler counters; in `exp33`–`exp35`, all `stage2_pre_install` ledgers are reconciled to record `(t-2, t [post-Stage2-step], t)`).*

### 1.2 Summary of Contributions

Across `208` multi-seed experimental arms (`623` total runs across Seeds `46, 47, 48`: **`145` three-seed strictly delayed zero-relay Delayed-WM arms [`435` runs]**, **`13` three-seed node-perturbation arms [`11` `SR-LNP` + `2` `SR-NP` = `39` runs]**, **`4` head/lexical-only backward-free anchors [`11` runs]**, **`3` three-seed same-clock compositional-relay diagnostic controls [`9` runs]**, **`7` three-seed local-pullback diagnostic controls [`21` runs]**, and **`36` three-seed backpropagation reference arms [`21` global BP + `5` block-skip surrogate BP + `10` sub-block direct-skip surrogate BP = `108` runs]** across `exp18, exp21, exp26`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp61`) on ClimbMix `d6` ($L=6, D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}, V=8,192$, $360$–$1,004$ steps $= 0.737\text{M}$–$2.056\text{M}$ tokens) and Native `50,332,176` ($L=8, D=512, V=8,192, T=2,048$, $100$ steps $= 13.1\text{M}$ tokens), where every Delayed-WM run executes inside `with torch.no_grad():` (`assert not torch.is_grad_enabled()`) with zero reverse matrix pullbacks, zero VJP helpers, and zero same-step relays, we establish seven results within the short-horizon developmental regime tested:

- **Reconciled Stream-Partitioned Estimator Analysis & Scale-Normalized Controls (Proposition 1, `exp26`, `exp35`, `exp44`, `exp58`, `exp60`):** We reconcile the exact implemented contraction $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}}(G; \text{scale\_factor}) = \frac{1}{N}\sum_{g=1}^G R_i^{(m, g)}(\text{scale\_factor}) Z_{i, :}^{(m, g)} = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}}(G)$ across both the default code convention `scale_factor = 1` (whose conditional mean scales as $1/M_g$ and covariance as $1/M_g^2$, giving mean $0.5$ on $N=4, G=2, M_g=2, a_n=x_n=1$) and the scale-normalized convention `scale_factor = M_g` (whose conditional mean $\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^N a_{n, i} x_n$ is invariant to $M_g$, giving $1.0$). We further distinguish **separable channels** (Stage 1: `mlp.c_fc, attn.c_v`, **Proposition 1(a–b)**, where first-order variance vanishes at $M_g = 1$) from **non-separable `RMSNorm` interfaces** (Stage 2: `x_final, x_0`, **Proposition 1(c)**), deriving the exact single-token ($M_g=1$) coordinate mean $\frac{a_{n, i}}{\text{rms}(z_n)}(1 - x_{n, i}^2/D) h_n$, cross-coordinate variance $\frac{a_{n, i}^2 x_{n, i}^2 (D - x_{n, i}^2)}{D^2 \text{rms}(z_n)^2} \|h_n\|_2^2$ (giving $0.064$ at $D=2, z=(1, 2)$), and radially projected modulation conditional mean $\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}] = \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)} P_{x_n}\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_{n, i}^2/D) a_n \neq \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)} P_{x_n} a_n = \mu_{\text{skip}, n}$ (verified at $D=3, x=(\sqrt{3/2}, \sqrt{3/2}, 0)^\top, a=(1, 0, 1)^\top$, where $\mathbb{E}[P_x\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}}^{(1)}] = (1/4, -1/4, 1)^\top \neq \mu_{\text{skip}} = (1/2, -1/2, 1)^\top$; its modulation-vector discrepancy is bounded by $\left(\frac{\max_i x_{n, i}^2}{D}\right)\frac{\|a_n\|_2}{\text{rms}(z_n)}$ and outer-product matrix Frobenius discrepancy by $\left(\frac{\max_i x_{n, i}^2}{D}\right)\frac{\|a_n\|_2 \|h_n\|_2}{\text{rms}(z_n)}$, where the normalized discrepancy coefficient $\max_i x_{n, i}^2 / D$ is $O(1/D)$ under a coordinate-concentration assumption $\max_i x_{n, i}^2 = O(1)$; furthermore, multiplying $\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}$ by the closed-form forward leverage inverse $\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_{n, i}^2/D)^{-1}$ prior to $P_{x_n}$ in `exp60` restores exact first-order unbiasedness $\cos = 1.000000$ at $D=3$ with zero extra probes and zero local pullbacks). Controlled 3-seed `scale_factor = M_g` runs (`exp35` on `d6` and `exp44` on Native `50.3M`) confirm that holding the conditional mean invariant to $M_g$ leaves learning curves virtually unchanged (`2.6097 -> 2.3361 -> 2.3008 -> 2.2338` for `scale_factor = M_g` vs. `2.5939 -> 2.3364 -> 2.2966 -> 2.2338` for `scale_factor = 1` across $G \in \{1, 64, 256, 2048\}$ on `d6`; `2.2539 ± 0.0013` vs. `2.2541 ± 0.0013` at $G=64$ under `Pre-Polar Muon`; and **`1.9350 ± 0.0003` for `scale_factor = 8.0` vs. `1.9358 ± 0.0002` for `scale_factor = 1.0` at $M_g=8, \text{lr\_embed}=3\text{e-}3$ on Native `50.3M`**, so along this tested intervention path **`96.9%` (`-0.0248` of `-0.0256` BPB) of the $M_g: 8 \to 1$ gain (`1.9350 -> 1.9102`) comes from eliminating $7/8$ within-stream cross-token interference terms**), establishing that variance reduction rather than raw step-scale changes drives the stream-partitioning gain.
- **Forward Causal Attention-Filtered Value Eligibility Trace (Proposition 2, `exp28`):** Because $v(b, s) = W_v x_{\text{attn\_in}}(b, s)$ commutes with causal attention over $s \le \tau$, computing the causal attention-filtered pre-synaptic trace $\bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}(b, \tau) = \sum_{s \le \tau} A_{b, h, \tau, s} x_{\text{attn\_in}}(b, s)$ during the forward pass and perturbing post-attention value outputs lifts single-step backward-free `attn.c_v` cosine alignment with the language-model cross-entropy loss gradient **$8.2\times$ from `+0.1104` to `+0.9069`**. Pairing $Z^{(2)}(t-1)$ with independent current-batch rewards $R^{(2)}(t)$ (`mismatched_origin_control`, `exp28`) degrades `d6` from `2.2338 ± 0.0102` to **`2.7608 ± 0.0056` DEV BPB (`-0.5270` BPB contrast)**.
- **Diagonal Skip-Routing Surrogate, Data-Dependent Kurtosis Expectation Bound, & Compliant Pure-`FP32` Clock/Optimizer Factorials (Propositions 3 & 4, `exp31`–`exp36`, `exp42`, `exp59`):** Routing lexical credit to `wte` via the diagonal skip surrogate $W_{\text{skip}} = \alpha_0 I_D$ (**Proposition 3**) cuts PyTorch `d6` rule time by **$27.4\%$ (`205.5 -> 149.2` ms/step)** with identical accuracy (`2.1579 ± 0.0004` vs. `2.1577 ± 0.0004`). For Strict Post-Installation Construction, **Proposition 4** decomposes $\delta W_{\text{lm}}(t-1)$ into weight decay, momentum history $\beta_1 m^{(t-2)}$, and current-batch gradient $(1-\beta_1) g^{(t-1)}$. Under row-isotropic preconditioning, reward-side projection $P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)}$ annihilates the $O(1)$ radial self-update vector $b_{\text{rad}}(n)$ identically, leaving an oblique `RMSNorm`-Jacobian coupling residual whose **expected** squared norm relative to $\|b_{\text{rad}}(n)\|_2^2$ is bounded by the **data-dependent coordinate fourth moment** $\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[\|P_x b\|_2^2]}{\|b_{\text{rad}}\|_2^2} \le \frac{\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}}(n))}{D}$ where $\kappa_4(x) := \frac{1}{D}\sum_{i=1}^D x_i^4$. We show both that unrestricted sparse vectors $x = \sqrt{D/5}(1, 2, 0, \dots, 0)$ have non-vanishing expectation ratio $0.0576$ ($\kappa_4(x)/D = 0.68$) for all $D \ge 2$, and that on a 20-step head/block-0 diagnostic trajectory on ClimbMix `d6` (`exp35` Part 0), $\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}})$ has token mean $2.9398$, yielding mean theoretical expectation bound $\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}})/D = 0.007656$ and empirical mean ratio $0.007458$ (with individual token maximum $0.124439$ across $40,960$ token evaluations). Across our **16-cell $2 \times 2 \times 2 \times 2$ Stage-2 Clock $\times$ Optimizer factorial (`exp34` + `exp35` Part B1)**, **4-cell True Both-Stages Pre-Install comparison (`exp35` Part B2)**, **compliant zero-pullback pure-`FP32` trace-cache Triton WM clock controls (`exp42` Part A)**, and **720-step 4-matrix and 6-matrix `both_stages_pre_install` vs. `strict_post_install` clock controls (`exp59` Arms 5–6)**:
  1. **Isolating Stage-1 vs. Stage-2 Clock Transitions, Compliant Pure-`FP32` Cache Controls, & 720-Step Clock Stability (`exp35` Part B2, `exp42` Part A, `exp59` Arms 5–6):** Moving Stage 1 from `(t-2, t-1 [pre-Stage2], t)` (`both_stages_pre_install`) to `(t-2, t [post-Stage2], t)` (`stage2_pre_install`) shifts `d6` DEV BPB by only **`+0.0013` to `+0.0024` BPB** across all 4 `entrywise` cells (`2.1341 -> 2.1360` at `b05, 1e-8`; `2.1195 ± 0.0005 -> 2.1217 ± 0.0006` at `b05, 1e-4`), consistent with update-orthogonalized `Pre-Polar Muon` inducing only a `4.04%` (`cos = 0.999238`) Stage-1 reward drift. Moving Stage 2 from `(t-1, t-1, t)` to `(t-1, t [post-Stage3], t)` at `(entrywise, b05, 1e-8)` shifts DEV BPB by **`+0.0219 ± 0.0020` BPB** (`2.1360 -> 2.1579`). Eliminating `BF16` trace caching in a compliant zero-pullback Triton WM trainer (`reverse_error_matmuls_per_step = 0`, `exp42_fp32_triton_results.json`) reproduces the exact same 3-seed clock contrast (**`2.1359 ± 0.0022` vs. `2.1579 ± 0.0004` DEV BPB** at `b05, 1e-8`, and **`2.1274 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB** at `b09, 1e-4`), confirming that the `+0.0219` BPB shift is a genuine receiver-installation effect rather than a `BF16` cache-rounding artifact (while `exp36` provides matching local-pullback diagnostic controls). Under the co-designed optimizer ($\epsilon = 10^{-4}, \text{lrm}: 1.0 \to 0.2$) at `720` steps (`exp59` Arms 5–6), `both_stages_pre_install` (`Stage 2: (t-1, t-1, t), Stage 1: (t-2, t-1, t)`) and `strict_post_install` (`(t-1, t, t)/(t-2, t, t)`) agree within **`0.0012`–`0.0020` BPB** (`1.9977 ± 0.0007` vs. `1.9989 ± 0.0007` on 4-matrix; `1.9692 ± 0.0005` vs. `1.9672 ± 0.0005` on 6-matrix $M=4$).
  2. **Row-Isotropic vs. Entrywise Preconditioning (`Proposition 4`, `exp34`–`exp35`):** Across all 4 `row_iso` cells of the 16-cell cube, Stage-2 Pre-Install and Strict Post-Install agree to within **`0.0003` BPB** (`2.1577` vs. `2.1580`; `2.1662` vs. `2.1659`; `2.1296` vs. `2.1298`; `2.1335` vs. `2.1334`). Under `entrywise` moments, increasing $\epsilon: 10^{-8} \to 10^{-4}$ damps coordinate spread in $d_{j, i} = (\sqrt{\hat{v}_{j, i}} + \epsilon)^{-1}$ and improves Strict Post-Install by **`-0.0279 ± 0.0001` BPB** (`2.1579 -> 2.1300`), while $(\beta_1=0.9, \epsilon=10^{-4})$ reaches **`2.1273 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB** (`2.0900 ± 0.0001` VAL-B).
- **Fused Register-Tiled Triton WM Kernels (`38.4x` Kernel Speedup & `8.33x` Steady-State Step Speedup on `d6`; Isolated `1.44x`–`6.33x` Steady-State Step Speedup & `2.63x` Implementation-Package Speedup on Native `50.3M`, `exp37`, `exp43`, `exp44`):** Building on the SRAM-fused outgoing-column contraction concept introduced in [5] (version 5), we specialize fused register-tiled Triton kernels (`_triton_wm_reward_mg1_kernel` for $M_g=1$ and `_triton_wm_reward_mg_grouped_kernel` for $M_g > 1$) for our 3-stage delayed perturbation-differential WM pipeline. Eliminating the 3D outer-product workspace (`3,498.9 MB` on `d6` `lm_head` at $G=2,048$) cuts the 13-matrix `d6` WM kernel suite **`38.4x` from `134.31 ms` to `3.50 ms/step`** at $G=2,048$ (`cos = 1.000000`, `rel_l2 = 2.26e-8` on `mlp.c_proj`) and **`4.4x` from `4.51 ms` to `1.03 ms/step`** at $G=64$ (`cos = 0.9999973`, `rel_l2 = 2.31e-3`). Combined with batched `Pre-Polar Muon`, steady-state `d6` `fwd + rule` step time at $G=2,048$ drops **`8.33x` from `155.6 ms` to `18.68 ± 0.21 ms/step`** (`6.72s` steady-state estimate for 360 steps; `6.74s`–`6.95s` measured 3-seed training-region GPU time). On Native `50.3M` ($B_{\text{eff}}=64, T=2,048$), `exp32`'s PyTorch baseline (`compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast`) processes stream chunks (`chunk_G=256`, allocating `4.62 GB` temporary chunk workspace; or `chunk_G=32`, allocating `0.62 GB`). In **genuinely isolated kernel substitutions** (`benchmark_native50m_isolated_kernel.json`, measuring within-run steady-state variation across steps `2–4`) holding all storage, precision, trace construction, optimizer execution, and timing boundaries fixed: (i) inside `exp32`'s CPU-offloaded trainer ($M_g=8$, after a `3`-step CPU-heap warmup), swapping only `compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast(chunk_G=256)` $\to$ `compute_stream_wm_rewards_triton_fast` speeds up the steady-state (`steps 2–4`) learning rule **`1.60x` (`6,456.78 ± 0.64 -> 4,039.89 ± 0.20 ms/step`)** and steady-state `fwd + rule` **`1.44x` (`7,876.97 ± 0.65 -> 5,460.10 ± 0.52 ms/step`, or `1.50x` rule and `1.35x` `fwd + rule` across all `5` steps `0–4` including pipeline startup)** while reducing peak VRAM from **`9.108 GiB` to `7.629 GiB`**; and (ii) inside `exp43`'s GPU-resident trainer (`20.57–20.65 GiB` peak VRAM, `2` warmup + `3` timed steady-state steps `2–4`, where `chunk_G=256` OOMs), swapping only `compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast(chunk_G=32)` $\to$ `compute_stream_wm_rewards_triton_fast` speeds up the 288-call contraction **`6.86x` (`2,363.30 ± 0.72 -> 344.60 ± 0.74 ms`)** and `fwd + rule` **`1.67x` (`5,055.97 ± 1.30 -> 3,033.91 ± 1.36 ms`)** at $M_g=8$, and speeds up the 288-call contraction **`29.54x` (`18,317.09 ± 20.32 -> 620.01 ± 0.22 ms`)**, the learning rule **`9.91x` (`19,689.83 ± 20.99 -> 1,987.43 ± 0.59 ms`)**, and `fwd + rule` **`6.33x` (`21,022.47 ± 21.62 -> 3,319.91 ± 0.59 ms`)** at $M_g=1$. Across 100-step 3-seed training runs (`exp43`–`exp44`), our **combined implementation package** (combining the Triton kernels with GPU-resident traces + pinned MLP ring buffers, `BF16` SDPA and deferred Stage-1 attention-filtered input reconstruction from `BF16` cached inputs, batched Muon orthogonalization, and in-place scatter head scoring, trading **`9.11 GiB -> 20.57 GiB` peak VRAM**—or **`9.61–9.69 GiB` peak VRAM** when all 8 delayed trace tensors are placed in pinned host ring buffers in `exp44`) matches `exp32` in quality (`1.9358 ± 0.0002` vs. `1.9361 ± 0.0004` DEV BPB at $M_g=8$) with a **`2.63x` speedup on matched `fwd + rule` timed regions (`7,857.05 -> 2,991.84 ms/step`, `3.88x` on the learning rule `6,432.04 -> 1,659.19 ms`)**, and reaches **`1.9102 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB** at $M_g=1, \text{lr\_embed}=3\text{e-}3$ and **`1.8978 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`1.8844 ± 0.0003` VAL-B BPB, `208.39` PPL)** at $M_g=1, \text{lr\_embed}=0.015, \epsilon=10^{-8}$ in `3.26 s/step` (`fwd + rule`; vs. `1.9266 ± 0.0004` at $\epsilon=10^{-4}$ in `exp44`).
- **Joint Co-Design of the Optimizer and Delayed WM Credit (`2.1273 -> 2.0682` DEV BPB at 360 Steps; `1.9989` at 720 Steps, `exp38`):** Adapting the `Pre-Polar Muon` foundation of [8] to delayed WM, we combine `Pre-Polar Muon` update orthogonalization with scale-matched sparse embedding velocity (`lr_wte = 0.12, lr_ve = 0.06, betas = (0.80, 0.95)`) and post-installation drift annealing (`lrm: 1.0 -> 0.2`) on `d6`. Across 3 seeds on `d6` (360 steps, Strict Post-Install), this optimizer package improves Delayed WM from `2.1273 ± 0.0001` to **`2.0682 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`2.0315 ± 0.0004` VAL-B BPB, `292.66` PPL)**—within **`0.0150` BPB** of matched-subset Exact Backpropagation (`2.0532 ± 0.0008`, which also improves from `2.1080 ± 0.0006` under the same optimizer package). On a Seed-46 Block-0 diagnostic audit (`run_geo_and_attn_steering_audit.py`), `Pre-Polar Muon` is descriptively associated with a higher `mlp.c_proj` weight stable rank than `AdamW` (`127.24` under `paper20_prepolar` and `141.33` under `codesigned_prepolar` vs. `2.75` under `adamw_hidden`), and the co-designed embedding/annealing schedule increases held-out attention-map Frobenius shift by $9.82\times$ in Layer 0 (`0.0838 -> 0.8233`) and $1.42\times$ across all 6 layers (`0.6046 -> 0.8587`).
- **Controlled Comparisons Against Author-Defined `SR-LNP` and `SR-NP` Node-Perturbation Recipes Across Tokens, Training-Region Time, and Width ($D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$, `exp37`, `exp39`, `exp42`, `exp56`):** We compare Delayed WM against two author-defined node-perturbation recipes on the matched 4-matrix `d6` subset: (i) **Surrogate-Routed Local Node Perturbation (`SR-LNP`, $K \in \{4, 16, 64\}$)**, which uses same-step terminal-skip routing ($g_{\text{bus}} \cdot \gamma_l$) and $\gamma = 0$ local value scoring; and (ii) **Suffix-Recomputed Node Perturbation (`SR-NP`, `exp42`)**, which uses Gaussian draw-mean centering, suffix recomputation from block $l$ onward, shared post-MLP block-output perturbations for `attn.c_proj` and `mlp.c_proj`, analytic `lm_head` gradients, and $\gamma = 0.98$ future-token value accumulation:
  1. **Equal Tokens (`360` steps on `d6`, $D=384$):** `Delayed WM (K=1, G=2048)` (`2.0682 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB, `6.74s` measured training-region GPU time / `6.72s` steady-state est.) outperforms `SR-LNP` at $K=4$ (`2.3071 ± 0.0016`, `6.32s` run / `5.48s` est.), $K=16$ (`2.2603 ± 0.0018`, `9.43s` run / `9.23s` est.), and $K=64$ (`2.2041 ± 0.0013`, `23.66s` run / `23.22s` est.), as well as 76-draw `SR-NP` (`2.2415 ± 0.0015` under `Pre-Polar Muon`, `65.19s` run; `2.4264 ± 0.0088` under `Adam`, `84.38s` run).
  2. **Matched Training-Region Time (`~9.4s` and `~13.6s` in the Specialized 4-Matrix `exp39` Trainer):** At `500` steps (`9.41s` run / `9.34s` est.), `Delayed WM` reaches **`2.0322 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`1.9975 ± 0.0006` VAL-B, `265.13` PPL)**. At `720` steps (`13.57s` run / `13.45s` est.), `Delayed WM` reaches **`1.9989 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`1.9646 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `241.96` PPL)**.
  3. **Model Width Scaling ($D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$, `exp39`, `exp56`, `exp59`, `exp60`):** As width $D = H \cdot d_h$ scales from `256 -> 384 -> 512 -> 768` at fixed head dimension $d_h = 64$, 4-matrix `Delayed WM`'s gap to 4-matrix Exact Backpropagation shrinks from `+0.0144` to **`+0.0125` BPB** (`2.0558 ± 0.0002` vs. `2.0433 ± 0.0006` at $D=768$). Furthermore, under 6-matrix $(W_Q, W_K)$ probing ($M=4$, `exp49`, `exp56`, `exp59`, `exp60`), Full 6-Matrix `Delayed WM` reaches **`2.0794 ± 0.0009` ($D=256$), `2.0552 ± 0.0007` ($D=384$; `2.0521 ± 0.0009` under `wiener_mmse` and `2.0519 ± 0.0009` under `wiener_mmse + Hadamard4` in `12.20s`), `2.0451 ± 0.0005` ($D=512$), and `2.0437 ± 0.0006` ($D=768$) DEV BPB**—reaching numerical proximity (**`-0.0013` to `+0.0020` DEV BPB**, rather than statistical equivalence) to the smaller 4-matrix Exact Backpropagation reference (`2.0789, 2.0532, 2.0442, 2.0433`, which leaves $W_Q, W_K$ frozen) while trailing the matched-subset 6-matrix Exact Backpropagation baseline (`2.0590, 2.0316, 2.0191, 2.0153`) by `+0.0203` to `+0.0284` DEV BPB—and Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=4$ (`24` SDPA probes/step: **`2.0814 ± 0.0010, 2.0562 ± 0.0010, 2.0467 ± 0.0012, 2.0454 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB**) captures **`85.6%`, `92.3%`, `88.1%`, and `85.9%`** of the full 6-block $M=4$ gain at half the SDPA probe overhead.
- **Three-Tier Attention Credit-Assignment Hierarchy ($W_V$ vs. $W_Q$ vs. $W_K$), Reward-Side Tangent Projection Variance (Proposition 5), Closed-Form Parameter-Free Wiener-MMSE Key Gather & Walsh-Hadamard $H_4$ Probe Multiplexing / Top-$K_{\text{echo}}=32$ Sparse Gather (Proposition 6, `2.0521 ± 0.0009` / `2.0519 ± 0.0009` at 360st; `1.9632 ± 0.0006` in `29.14s` [dense] & `1.9633 ± 0.0009` in `24.36s` [`topk32`] at 720st, `exp59`, `exp60`), Decoupled 6-Matrix & Selective Upper-Half Routing across Fixed Tokens (`360, 720` steps), Fixed-Probe Reallocation (`720`st Upper-Half $M=4$: `1.9699 ± 0.0001` in `21.05s` [`g085`] & `1.9672 ± 0.0017` in `22.36s` [`wiener_mmse`]), and Same-Trainer Matched Training-Region Time (`740`st Upper-Half $M=4$: `1.9670 ± 0.0002` in `19.19s`; `840`st Upper-Half $M=2$: `1.9609 ± 0.0006` in `21.71s` vs. `1,004`st 4-Matrix `1.9720 ± 0.0006` in `21.55s`, `exp45`, `exp47`, `exp49`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp54`, `exp56`, `exp59`, `exp60`) & Native `50.3M` (`1.8978 -> 1.8884 ± 0.0007` [6-Mat $M=1$], `1.8881 ± 0.0010` [Upper-Half `(4..7)` $M=2, \gamma_K=0.85$], `1.8858 ± 0.0011` [Upper-Half `(4..7)` $M=4, \gamma_K=0.85$], `1.8853 ± 0.0013` [6-Mat $M=2, \gamma_K=0.85$], `1.8849 ± 0.0003` [Upper-Half `(4..7)` $M=2$ `Wiener-MMSE` in `5.56s/step`], & **`1.8803 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB / `1.8668 ± 0.0008` VAL-B / `198.42` PPL [Upper-Half `(4..7)` $M=4$ `Wiener-MMSE + Hadamard-4` in `6.29s/step, 10.06 GiB` VRAM]**, `exp46`, `exp48`, `exp55`, `exp61`):** Building on the separate attention-internal jittering (including gates), per-head attention-output alignment, token-local query credit ($\gamma = 0$), and $\gamma$-discounted future-token key credit of Dust [9] (`Section 2.3`) and concurrent `general_pretraining_18/v8`–`v10` [5], we evaluate attention-routing credit assignment in our 3-stage delayed WM pipeline (`exp45`–`exp61`). Conditioned on fixed pre-attention activations and oracle downstream $\nabla_{y_{\text{attn}}}\mathcal{L}$ (`gy_attn` from `loss.backward()` at initialized `seed = 46`, Layer 0): (i) **Tier 1 (`attn.c_v`, $m_{\text{eff}} = 1$, coordinate-isolated & forward-factorizable via $\bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}$)** satisfies a conditional single-block reconstruction identity **`cos = 1.0000`** at $K=1$ (distinct from its `+0.9069` end-to-end delayed backward-free cosine in Table 1); (ii) **Tier 2 (`attn.c_q`, $m_{\text{eff}} = d_h$, token-local in time $\tau$ because query $q_\tau^{(h)}$ enters only row $\tau$ of $\mathbf{A}^{(h)}$, but coupled across the $d_h$ head channels inside $\langle q_\tau^{(h)}, k_s^{(h)}\rangle / \sqrt{d_h}$)** achieves empirical single-draw cosine **`cos = 0.1357`** at this initialized diagnostic state (comparable to $1/\sqrt{64} = 0.1250$), scaling with draw count $K$ to `0.2657` ($K=4$), `0.4748` ($K=16$), and `0.7385` ($K=64$); and (iii) **Tier 3 (`attn.c_k`, non-local across future queries $\tau \ge s$)** satisfies the conditional first-order expectation identity $\mathbb{E}[\widehat{g}_{k_s}^{(\gamma_K)}] = \sum_{\tau \ge s} \gamma_K^{\tau - s} J_{\tau s}^\top g_\tau + O(\sigma)$: same-position correlation ($\gamma_K = 0$) captures only $J_{ss}^\top g_s$ while truncating $\sum_{\tau > s} J_{\tau s}^\top g_\tau$ (`cos = 0.0076` at $K=1$, `0.0534` at $K=64$), whereas accumulating future query rewards $\widetilde{R}_{k,s}^{(\gamma_K)} = \sum_{\tau \ge s, \tau - s \le W_l} \gamma_K^{\tau - s} R_{\text{head},\tau}$ restores all future terms at $\gamma_K = 1$ (illustrated by our 2-token finite-difference Rademacher enumeration with `eps_fd = 1e-5`, `tol = 1e-9`) and trades a truncation bias whose size depends on the omitted future sensitivity mass at $\gamma_K \in \{0.85, 0.98\}$ for lower cross-time key superposition variance (`K=64` cosine: `0.0534` at $\gamma_K=0$, `0.1329` at $\gamma_K=0.85$, `0.1565` at $\gamma_K=0.98$, `0.1340` at $\gamma_K=1.00$). Furthermore, **Proposition 5** proves that for a normalized head vector $u \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h}$ ($\|u\|_2^2 = d_h$) and fixed tangent sensitivity $g \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h}$ ($g^\top u = 0$) expressed in the coordinate basis where $\xi \in \{-1, +1\}^{d_h}$ has independent Rademacher signs (specifically, the **pre-rotary coordinate frame** $(u_{\text{pre}}, g_{\text{pre}}) = (R_\theta^\top u_{\text{post}}, R_\theta^\top g_{\text{post}})$ when signs are drawn before orthogonal RoPE $R_\theta$), while forward `RMSNorm` removes radial perturbations to first order, unprojected reconstruction $\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi) = (g^\top \xi)\xi$ has conditional covariance trace exceeding tangent-projected reconstruction $\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}(\xi) = P_u \hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi)$ by the exact fourth-moment difference $\Delta = \operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}(\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}) - \operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}(\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}) = \frac{1}{d_h}\mathbb{E}[(g^\top \xi)^2 (u^\top \xi)^2] = \frac{1}{d_h}\sum_{1 \le i < j \le d_h}(u_i g_j + u_j g_i)^2 = \|g\|_2^2 - \frac{2}{d_h}\sum_{i=1}^{d_h} u_i^2 g_i^2 \ge 0$ (giving $\Delta = 1$ on $d_h=2, u=(\sqrt(2), 0)^\top, g=(0, 1)^\top$ and $d_h=4, u=(2, 0, 0, 0)^\top, g=(0, 1, 0, 0)^\top$, and $\Delta = 0$ on the boundary $d_h=2, u=(1, 1)^\top, g=(1, -1)^\top$). Going beyond fixed recency discounting $\gamma_K^{\tau-s}$, **Proposition 6 (`exp59`–`exp61`)** analyzes general causal query-to-key gather weights $W_{\tau, s}$ in $\widetilde{R}_{k, s}^{(W)} = \sum_{\tau \ge s} W_{\tau, s} R_{\text{head}, \tau}$: because the forward softmax perturbation $k_{s'} \mapsto k_{s'} + \sigma \tilde{\xi}_{k, s'}$ already scales key $s$'s contribution inside query $\tau$'s scalar reward by $A_{\tau, s}$ ($\mathbb{E}[R_{\text{head}, \tau}\tilde{\xi}_{k, s}/\sigma] = A_{\tau, s} c_{\tau, s} = J_{\tau s}^\top g_\tau$), naive attention-weighted (`Rake`) gathering $W_{\tau, s} = A_{\tau, s}$ weights each future query by $A_{\tau, s}^2$ in expectation, under-weighting moderate attention links once heads sharpen over training. Solving for the per-pair linear minimum-MSE shrinkage weight under isotropic sensitivity prior across $M$ averaged Rademacher probes yields the closed-form parameter-free **Wiener-MMSE Key-Gather filter** $W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(M) = \frac{M A_{\tau, s}^2}{(M - 1)A_{\tau, s}^2 + \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2} \in [0, 1]$, which approaches $1$ on dominant links ($A_{\tau, s} \to 1$, eliminating the $A_{\tau, s}^2$ squaring bias) while suppressing cross-key superposition noise ($W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}} \to 0$) when query $\tau$ attends elsewhere ($A_{\tau, s} \to 0$). On `d6` ($M=4$, `exp59` Part 1), moving from $\gamma_K = 0.85$ $\to$ `rake_attn` ($W_{\tau, s} = A_{\tau, s}$) $\to$ `wiener_mmse` ($W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(4)$) increases 6-block mean key cosine alignment **`2.98x` at Step 0 (`0.0317 -> 0.0870 -> 0.0945`) and `3.22x` at Step 120 (`0.0284 -> 0.0727 -> 0.0914`, and `3.38x` on Upper-Half `3..5`: `0.0223 -> 0.0618 -> 0.0753`)**. Under the exact `exp49` update law (`exact_exp49_opt=True`, $\eta_K = 0.024$, `exp59` Arms 7–12 and `exp60` Arms 1–6), **360-step Full 6-Matrix $M=4$ improves from `2.0552 ± 0.0007` ($\gamma_K=0.85$) to `2.0529 ± 0.0003` (`rake_attn`), `2.0521 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB (`2.0151 ± 0.0010` VAL-B, `280.04` PPL in `14.71s`, dense `wiener_mmse`), `2.0522 ± 0.0010` (`2.0152 ± 0.0012` VAL-B in `12.16s`, sparse Top-$K_{\text{echo}}=32$ `wiener_mmse`, which retains `99.77%` of $\|W^{\text{MMSE}}\|_F^2$ at Step 120), and `2.0519 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB (`2.0147 ± 0.0009` VAL-B, `279.83` PPL in `12.20s`, `wiener_mmse + Hadamard4` 4-channel temporal probe multiplexing—recovering `66.2%` [`-0.0163 / -0.0216` BPB] of the 360-step 4-to-6-matrix Exact-BP gain and reaching `-0.0013` BPB below the 4-matrix Exact-BP reference `2.0532 ± 0.0008` while trailing 6-matrix Exact BP `2.0316 ± 0.0009` by `+0.0203` BPB; Upper-Half `3..5` $M=4$ `wiener_mmse` reaches `2.0540 ± 0.0010` in `11.18s`)**; and at **720 steps**, **Full 6-Matrix $M=4$ improves from `1.9672 ± 0.0005` ($\gamma_K=0.85$) to `1.9658 ± 0.0010` (`rake_attn`, `28.97s`), `1.9632 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`1.9284 ± 0.0006` VAL-B, `219.39` PPL in `29.14s`, dense `wiener_mmse`), `1.9633 ± 0.0009` (`1.9285 ± 0.0009` VAL-B, `219.42` PPL in `24.36s`, sparse Top-$K_{\text{echo}}=32$ `wiener_mmse`—matching dense `wiener_mmse` within `0.0001` BPB while cutting step time by `16.4%`), and `1.9632 ± 0.0009` (`1.9283 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `219.37` PPL in `24.54s`, `wiener_mmse + Hadamard4 + Stage-2 leverage inversion`)—outperforming $\gamma_K=0.85$ on every seed by `-0.0040 ± 0.0002` BPB and recovering `68.8%` [`-0.0357 / -0.0519` BPB] of the 720-step 4-to-6-matrix Exact-BP gain; Upper-Half `3..5` $M=4$ `wiener_mmse` reaches `1.9672 ± 0.0017` in `22.36s`, matching Full 6-Block $M=4$ $\gamma_K=0.85$ at half the SDPA probes**. On 5-matrix ClimbMix `d6` (`25,460,736 / 26,346,060 = 96.64%` active parameters, `exp45`), **Spherical Pre-Polar Muon** for `attn.c_q` ($\beta_Q = 0.95, \eta_Q = 0.048$) improves Delayed WM from `2.0682 ± 0.0006` to **`2.0641 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`289.43` PPL)**, while both the tangent-projected (`7.41s`) and unprojected (`13.69s`) Euclidean controls at matched $\beta_Q=0.95, \eta_Q=0.048$ reach **`2.0649 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB** (isolating `-0.0033` BPB from gain/momentum matching and `-0.0008` BPB from spherical retraction + unit-RMS perturbation). Extending to **all 6 principal hidden matrix families** on `d6` (`26,345,472 / 26,346,060 = 99.9978%` active parameters, where the `588` frozen entries consist of `576` `ve_gate` weights of shape $6 \times 32$ at zero-based blocks `1, 3, 5` plus `12` residual scalars; and `26,346,048 / 26,346,060 = 99.99995%` = `100%` of matrix weights when `ve_gate` is also unfrozen in `exp49`, leaving only the `12` scalars frozen) resolves `exp45`'s coupled same-position degradation (`2.0669`) via **decoupled forward query/key displacement probes** (`-0.0014` BPB) and **discounted future-query key credit** ($\gamma_K = 0.85, \eta_K = 0.012, \beta_K = 0.98$, `-0.0015` BPB) to reach **`2.0631 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`288.64` PPL, `8.22s`)** at $M=1$. Averaging $M$ independent local query/key Rademacher probes inside $\bar{u}_q, \bar{u}_k$ on the same clean forward trunk prior to the single **Stage-1** `bmm` and Muon step reaches **`2.0596 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB** (`2.0595 ± 0.0007` with isolated RNG, `9.35s`; `2.0598 ± 0.0005` without reward-side projection, `10.72s`; and an exploratory **`2.0591 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB** with active `ve_gate`, `11.12s`, paired difference $-0.00047 \pm 0.00064$ BPB, recovering `40.3%` [`0.0087 / 0.0216`] of the 360-step 4-to-6-matrix exact-BP gain `2.0532 -> 2.0316`) at $M=2$, and **`2.0552 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`2.0183 ± 0.0005` VAL-B, `282.43` PPL, `13.94s`, recovering `60.2%` of the 360-step 4-to-6-matrix BP gain)** at $M=4$ (with Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=4$ reaching **`2.0562 ± 0.0010` DEV BPB in `9.12s`**, capturing `92.3%` of the full 6-block $M=4$ gain at half the SDPA probes in `exp56`). At `720` fixed steps (`1.475M` tokens, `exp49`, `exp51`, `exp53`), Exact Backpropagation's 4-to-6-matrix gain widens $2.40\times$ from `-0.0216` to **`-0.0519` BPB (`1.9423 ± 0.0031 -> 1.8904 ± 0.0012` DEV BPB)**, and backward-free 6-matrix Delayed WM recovers **`43.5%`–`44.9%` (`-0.0226` to `-0.0233` BPB: `1.9989 -> 1.9763 ± 0.0009` [`exp49`, `21.67s`] and `1.9756 ± 0.0017` [`exp53` main-RNG-matched control, `21.77s`]) at $M=2$**, **`59.0%` (`-0.0306` BPB: `1.9989 -> 1.9683 ± 0.0007` in `28.57s`, `exp51`) at $M=4 + \text{ve\_gate}$**, **`61.1%` (`-0.0317` BPB: `1.9989 -> 1.9672 ± 0.0005` DEV, `1.9324 ± 0.0005` VAL-B, `221.80` PPL in `27.71s`, `exp53`) at pure $M=4$ ($\gamma_K=0.85$)**, and **`68.8%` (`-0.0357` BPB: `1.9989 -> 1.9632 ± 0.0006` DEV, `1.9284 ± 0.0006` VAL-B, `219.39` PPL in `29.14s` [`exp59` dense `wiener_mmse`] and `1.9633 ± 0.0009` DEV in `24.36s` [`exp60` sparse `topk32` `wiener_mmse`])**. Crucially, evaluating **same-trainer matched training-region GPU time** (`Fwd + Rule`, `exp49`–`exp54`) and our **main model/data RNG-stream-matched and per-layer-RNG-synchronized 720-step $2 \times 2$ `{Lower-Half (0,1,2), Upper-Half (3,4,5)}` depth factorials** (`exp51`, `exp53` Arm 1, `exp56` Arms 11–12) reveals why uniform 6-block probing loses to 4-matrix WM at matched time (`1.9720 ± 0.0006` at 1,004 steps in `21.55s` vs. `1.9763 ± 0.0009` / `1.9756 ± 0.0017` at 720 steps in `21.67s` / `21.77s`): Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=2$ (`1.9779 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB in `18.59s`; and **`1.9769 ± 0.0021` DEV BPB in `16.09s`** when per-layer query/key RNG draws are also synchronized across skipped blocks in `exp56`) captures **`90.1%`–`94.4%` (`-0.0210` to `-0.0220` of `-0.0233`/`-0.0226` BPB)** of the full 6-block gain at half the SDPA probe overhead, whereas Lower-Half `(0, 1, 2)` $M=2$ (`1.9832 ± 0.0008` in `18.90s`; `1.9842 ± 0.0014` in `16.06s` layer-RNG-synced) captures `63.1%`–`67.4%` and adding Lower-Half `(0, 1, 2)` probes on top of Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` contributes only `-0.0013` to `-0.0023` BPB while costing `+3.08s–3.18s`. Consequently, reallocating the **exact same 24 perturbed SDPA calls/step** at **fixed `720` steps** (`exp53` Arm 3: Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=4$, `M=0` on `0, 1, 2`) reaches **`1.9699 ± 0.0001` DEV BPB (`1.9349 ± 0.0001` VAL-B, `223.42` PPL) in `21.05s`**—beating both uniform 720-step Full 6-Block $M=2$ (`1.9756` / `1.9763`) by `-0.0057` to `-0.0064` DEV BPB at equal tokens and 1,004-step 4-matrix (`1.9720 ± 0.0006` in `21.55s`) by `-0.0021` DEV BPB (`-0.0043` VAL-B) in `0.50s` less time; extending Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=4$ to `740` steps (`exp54` Arm 3, `19.19s`) reaches **`1.9670 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB (`1.9321 ± 0.0002` VAL-B, `221.69` PPL)**; and at the **matched `21.55s–21.71s` training-region time budget** (`exp51`), **840-step Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=2$ (`1.9609 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB, `1.9264 ± 0.0007` VAL-B BPB, `218.03` PPL in `21.71s`) beats 1,004-step 4-Matrix Delayed WM (`1.9720 ± 0.0006` in `21.55s`) by `-0.0111` DEV BPB (`-0.0128` VAL-B BPB, `-6.70` PPL)**. Furthermore, controlled Stage-2 bus multi-probe (`M_bus = 2`, `1.9700 ± 0.0002` vs. `1.9699 ± 0.0001`) and downstream-reader compositional credit ablations (`exp53` Arm 4, `exp54` Arms 1–2, 4: `1.9779`–`1.9787` vs. `1.9779`) empirically confirm Proposition 1(c)'s $O(1/D)$ Stage-2 terminal-bus SNR and show that single-hop terminal-skip credit already saturates Stage-2 writer learning without two-hop compositional variance. At a fixed 100-step ($13.1\text{M}$-token) budget on **Native `50,332,176`** ($L=8, D=512, T=2,048, M_g=1, \eta_Q = 0.048$): 5-matrix Spherical Active-$W_Q$ (`exp46`, `95.8%` active parameters) reaches **`1.8897 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`203.72` PPL)** in `6,012.9 ms/step` and `9.80 GiB` VRAM; **Upper-Half `(4, 5, 6, 7)` Decoupled Active-$(W_Q, W_K)$ (`exp55`, `exp61`, `48.23M` active parameters, `9.96–10.09 GiB` VRAM)** reaches **`1.8881 ± 0.0010` DEV BPB (`1.8750 ± 0.0010` VAL-B, `202.80` PPL)** at $M=2, \gamma_K=0.85$ (`16` perturbed SDPA calls/mb, `5,679.7 ms/step`), **`1.8858 ± 0.0011` DEV BPB (`1.8727 ± 0.0012` VAL-B, `201.54` PPL)** at $M=4, \gamma_K=0.85$ (`32` perturbed SDPA calls/mb, `6,568.7 ms/step`—retaining **`96.0%` [`-0.0120 / -0.0125` BPB]** of Full 8-Block $M=2$ $\gamma_K=0.85$'s `1.8853 ± 0.0013` gain with lower blocks `0..3` $(W_Q, W_K)$ frozen), **`1.8849 ± 0.0003` DEV BPB (`1.8717 ± 0.0005` VAL-B, `200.99` PPL)** at $M=2$ under **closed-form `Wiener-MMSE` key gathering** (`exp61` Arm 1, `16` SDPA calls/mb, `5,559.1 ms/step`, `9.96 GiB` VRAM—**outperforming Full 8-Block $M=2$ $\gamma_K=0.85$ `1.8853 ± 0.0013` at half the SDPA probes and improving over Upper-Half $M=2$ $\gamma_K=0.85$ by `-0.0032` BPB**), and **`1.8803 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`1.8668 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `198.42` PPL — `1.8801 / 1.8797 / 1.8811`)** at $M=4$ under **`Wiener-MMSE + Hadamard-4` temporal probe multiplexing** (`exp61` Arm 2, `32` SDPA calls/mb, `6,293.7 ms/step`, `10.06 GiB` VRAM—**breaking the `200` DEV PPL barrier backward-free, improving over Upper-Half $M=4$ $\gamma_K=0.85$ by `-0.0055` BPB, improving over Full 8-Block $M=2$ $\gamma_K=0.85$ by `-0.0050` BPB at the same 32-SDPA-probe budget, and widening the fixed-token gain over 4-matrix `exp43` `1.8978 ± 0.0007` by `1.40x` to `-0.0175` DEV BPB / `-0.0176` VAL-B BPB / `-9.97` PPL**).

---

## 2. Related Work and Methodological Distinctions

**Weight Maximization, Unbiased WM, and Node-Perturbation Methods (Dust).** Chung [1] introduced Weight Maximization (WM) for networks of self-interested reinforcement learning agents (*Learning by Competition of Self-Interested Reinforcement Learning Agents*, arXiv:2010.09770), proving that when each unit modulates its eligibility trace by the change in squared Euclidean norm of its outgoing weights, expected updates follow the gradient of downstream weight magnitude without backward error transport. Chung [2] subsequently formulated *Unbiased Weight Maximization* (UWM, arXiv:2307.13270) for Bernoulli-logistic units using an unbiased outgoing-weight product estimator. These external papers establish the original WM learning principle; they do not provide an inherited convergence guarantee for our multi-token perturbation-differential surrogate or diagonal skip routing. Recently, Dahal et al. [9] introduced *Dust* (*Pretraining Transformers Without Backpropagation*, QLabs, October 2026), which trains Transformer language models without backpropagation by injecting $K$ tokenwise Gaussian activation perturbation draws across separate layer passes, recomputing the affected network suffix from cached prefixes, rewarding direct layers via draw-centered token-loss reductions ($\gamma = 0$), and rewarding attention internals (`Section 2.3` of [9]) via separate per-role jittering (including attention gates) and per-head local attention-output alignment—scoring queries token-locally ($\gamma = 0$) because each query affects only its own causal row, and scoring keys/values by accumulating future query-token rewards ($\gamma = 0.98$, plus $49\text{k}$–$65\text{k}$ vocabulary-slab head draws and ES on residual scalars). We explicitly credit Dust [9] (`Section 2.3`) for separate attention-internal probes (including gates), per-head attention-output rewards, token-local query vs. future-token key/value credit, and averaging independent local estimates. Because our node-perturbation comparators and baseline experiments train the 4-matrix subset (`mlp.c_fc, mlp.c_proj, attn.c_v, attn.c_proj` plus `wte, value_embeds, lm_head`) at small per-step draw counts rather than replicating [9]'s 65k-head-slab, $K \in [64, 16384]$ suffix-recomputation protocol, **we do not claim head-to-head superiority over published Dust [9]**. Instead, we evaluate two author-defined node-perturbation comparators on our matched 4-matrix architecture: (i) **Surrogate-Routed Local Node Perturbation (`SR-LNP`, `exp37`–`exp39`)**, which shares the terminal-skip residual factors ($\gamma_l, \alpha_0$) of our Delayed WM pipeline but differs in credit timing (constructing $\hat{g}_{\text{bus}}$ and immediately using it for same-step embedding and hidden-layer estimates rather than 3-stage delayed packets), perturbation location, and attention-credit treatment (scoring value perturbations locally with $\gamma = 0$ rather than via the forward attention-filtered trace); and (ii) **Suffix-Recomputed Node Perturbation (`SR-NP`, `exp42`)**, an author-defined suffix-recomputed recipe that uses Gaussian draw-mean centering, suffix recomputation from block $l+1$ onward, shared post-MLP block-output perturbations $\hat{g}_{\text{wr}}$ for both `attn.c_proj` and `mlp.c_proj` (which omits `attn.c_proj`'s path through the same block's MLP), an analytic local loss gradient on `lm_head`, and $\gamma = 0.98$ future-token accumulation on per-head attention-output alignment.

**Concurrent rStation Studies on Forward-Only and Delayed Transformer Credit Assignment.** Three concurrent rStation papers independently investigate delayed and global-backward-free WM on `general_pretraining`:
- In `general_pretraining_18/v7`–`v10` [5] and `general_pretraining_21/v1`–`v2` [10], `cartan_transport` independently develops token micro-groups (stream partitioning), column-isolated WM rewards, the replacement of sibling-unit interference by within-group token interference, exact symmetrized unordered-pair conditional covariance formulas, and SRAM-fused outgoing-column contractions (`Delayed-Trace-Co-WM-S`) combined with $P=64$ and $P=256$ suffix forward probes for attention on ClimbMix `d6` (`2.04412 ± 0.00014` BPB at $P=64$ in $633.6\text{s}/635.2\text{s}$, `2.08637 ± 0.01282` at $P=256$, and `2.14471 ± 0.00464` at $1,000$ steps), 2-seed (`s42` and `s137`) 200-step recency-weighted value-pooling diagnostics (`2.14681` and `2.13220` BPB at $\gamma = 1.00$, `2.13081` at $\gamma = 0.98$), $P$-probe local attention credit assignment (`v8`–`v10`) with token-local query scoring ($\gamma = 0$), discounted future-token key/value accumulation ($\gamma = 0.98$), and upper-block ($l \ge L/2$) writer perturbation scaling (`wsig04`), and (in `general_pretraining_21/v1`–`v2` [10]) residual-highway input-bus pooling, the Diagonal-Attention DAG Theorem / Three-Level Causal Credit Hierarchy, and shared cross-slab Rademacher-Hadamard value multiplexing (`2.07883` and `2.06198` [+OE] 200-step BPB on `d6`, `2.06742` at $D=512$, and `2.01977` at $T=2,048$). Likewise, [8] (`general_pretraining_12/v2`) supplies the `Pre-Polar Muon` optimizer foundation. We explicitly credit [5] and [10] for the micro-group, symmetrized pair-covariance, SRAM-fused outgoing-column contraction, local attention-probe, upper-block credit-sensitivity, and Hadamard probe-multiplexing foundations and [8] for `Pre-Polar Muon`. Our distinct contributions are: (i) reconciling the code-level $1/M_g$ normalization with 3-seed scale-normalized controls on both `d6` (`exp35`) and Native `50.3M` (`exp44`) and deriving the single-token cross-coordinate variance and closed-form leverage inversion $\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_{n,i}^2/D)^{-1}$ at non-separable `RMSNorm` interfaces (**Proposition 1(a–c)**, `exp58`, `exp60`); (ii) training `attn.c_v` via a **forward causal attention-filtered pre-synaptic trace** $\bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}$ (**Proposition 2**) without multi-probe value suffix sweeps; (iii) routing lexical credit across the diagonal skip surrogate $W_{\text{skip}} = \alpha_0 I_D$ (**Proposition 3**); (iv) isolating Stage-1 vs. Stage-2 post-installation drift, reward-side tangent projection variance, and closed-form Wiener-MMSE key gathering, rotary-pair-aware Walsh-Hadamard $H_4$ query/key probe multiplexing, and sparse Top-$K_{\text{echo}}=32$ key gathering (**Propositions 4–6**, `exp32`–`exp35`, `exp42`, `exp49`, `exp59`–`exp61`); and (v) evaluating 6-matrix decoupled $(W_Q, W_K)$ probing ($M \in \{1, 2, 4\}$ across widths $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$), exploratory active `ve_gate` vs. pure $M=4$ controls, main-RNG-matched and layer-RNG-synchronized $2 \times 2$ depth factorials, fixed-probe-budget Upper-Half $M=4$ reallocation (`1.9699 ± 0.0001` at 720 steps [`g085`], `1.9672 ± 0.0017` at 720 steps [`wiener_mmse`]; `1.9670 ± 0.0002` at 740 steps [`g085`]), Full 6-Matrix $M=4$ Wiener-MMSE key gathering (`2.0521 ± 0.0009` / `2.0519 ± 0.0009` at 360 steps; `1.9632 ± 0.0006` [dense] & `1.9633 ± 0.0009` [sparse `topk32` in `24.36s`] at 720 steps on `d6`, and `1.8849 ± 0.0003` [$M=2$] / **`1.8803 ± 0.0007` [$M=4$, `198.42` PPL]** on Native `50.3M`), and same-trainer matched-training-region-time 4-matrix vs. selective Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` controls (`1.9609 ± 0.0006` vs. `1.9720 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB in `21.71s` vs. `21.55s`, `exp47`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp56`, `exp59`–`exp61`).
- In `general_pretraining_19/v4`–`v6`, `residual_cartographer` [6] analyzes short residual writer/reader shortcuts, two-world delayed WM (`1.98964` DEV BPB at $499.65\text{M}$ targets, `2.00286` at $134.2\text{M}$ targets on Native `50.3M` in `v4`), sequence-wide incoming-weight actions with previous-origin activity directions (`2.1184/2.0975` DEV BPB at $134.2\text{M}$ targets on Native `50.3M` in `v5`), and four-world role coding vs. two-world action persistence across depths 8 and 12 (`v6`). Because [5], [6], [9], and [10] use different token budgets and schedules than our studies, raw BPB endpoints across [5], [6], [9], and [10] are not direct head-to-head comparisons; rather, our work complements [6] by commuting $W_v^{(h)}$ with causal attention during the forward pass (**Proposition 2**), characterizing post-installation drift (**Proposition 4**), and testing `residual_cartographer`'s realized-component reader hypothesis in `exp36` Part B.

---

## 3. Mathematical Formulation and Estimator Analysis

### 3.1 Three-Stage Residual Pipeline

Consider an $L$-layer causal Transformer with width $D$, vocabulary size $V$, token embedding $e_{\text{tok}}(x) = W_{\text{wte}}[x]$, initial normalized state $x_0 = \text{RMSNorm}(e_{\text{tok}}(x))$, per-layer residual recurrence $x_{\text{in}}^{(l)} = \lambda_{\text{res}}^{(l)} x_{\text{out}}^{(l-1)} + \lambda_{x_0}^{(l)} x_0$, attention and MLP sublayers writing additively via $W_{\text{attn\_proj}}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times D}$ and $W_{\text{mlp\_proj}}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times 4D}$, terminal state $x_{\text{final}} = \text{RMSNorm}(x_L)$, and softcapped readout logits $z = 15 \tanh(W_{\text{lm}} x_{\text{final}} / 15)$ with $W_{\text{lm}} \in \mathbb{R}^{V \times D}$. Unrolling the linear residual scalings $\lambda_{\text{res}}^{(l)}, \lambda_{x_0}^{(l)}$ yields the exact terminal representation
$$
x_L = \alpha_0 x_0 + \sum_{l=0}^{L-1} \gamma_l \left(W_{\text{attn\_proj}}^{(l)} y_{\text{attn}}^{(l)} + W_{\text{mlp\_proj}}^{(l)} h_{\text{mlp}}^{(l)}\right), \qquad \gamma_l = \prod_{m=l+1}^{L-1} \lambda_{\text{res}}^{(m)}, \quad \alpha_0 = \prod_{m=0}^{L-1} \lambda_{\text{res}}^{(m)} + \sum_{l=0}^{L-1} \lambda_{x_0}^{(l)} \gamma_l.
$$
Exploiting these direct additive write paths into $x_L$, we partition all trainable parameters into three causal stages with maximum pipeline delay $2$ independent of depth $L$:
- **Stage 3 (`Delay = 0`, Terminal Readout):** $W_{\text{lm}} \in \mathbb{R}^{V \times D}$.
- **Stage 2 (`Delay = 1`, Residual Stream Writers):** $W_{\text{wte}} \in \mathbb{R}^{V \times D}$, $W_{\text{mlp\_proj}}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times 4D}$, and $W_{\text{attn\_proj}}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times D}$ for $l \in \{0, \dots, L-1\}$, whose outgoing receiver columns are $W_{\text{lm}}[:, i] \in \mathbb{R}^V$.
- **Stage 1 (`Delay = 2`, Internal Sublayer Writers):** $W_{\text{mlp\_fc}}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{4D \times D}$, $W_{v}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times D}$, $W_{\text{ve}}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{V \times D}$, and (in our 5-matrix and 6-matrix configurations, `exp45`–`exp48`) $W_{q}^{(l)}, W_{k}^{(l)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times D}$, whose outgoing receiver columns are $W_{\text{mlp\_proj}}^{(l)}[:, p] \in \mathbb{R}^D$ and $W_{\text{attn\_proj}}^{(l)}[:, p] \in \mathbb{R}^D$.

### 3.2 Reconciled Code Contraction, Separable Pair Covariance, and `RMSNorm` Interface Moments (`Proposition 1`)

Let $N = BT$ denote the total number of tokens in a batch, and partition $\{1, \dots, N\} = \bigsqcup_{g=1}^G \mathcal{S}_g$ into $G$ disjoint streams of size $M_g = N / G$ tokens each. During the forward pass, unit $i$ at stage $m$ injects i.i.d. Rademacher exploration noise $\xi_{n, i} \sim \text{Unif}(\{\pm 1\})$ with scale $\sigma > 0$, and records the stream eligibility trace $Z_{i, :}^{(m, g)} = \sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} (\xi_{n, i}/\sigma) x_n \in \mathbb{R}^{D_{\text{in}}}$.

In `compute_stream_wm_rewards_chunked` (`exp26`) and `compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast` (`exp32`–`exp35`), the stream reward divides by $M_g$ with an optional multiplier `scale_factor` (`mult = scale_factor / M_g`):
$$
R_i^{(m, g)}(\text{scale\_factor}) = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \sum_{n' \in \mathcal{S}_g} u_{n'}^{(m+1), T} W_{:, i}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}}) \, \Delta y_{n', i}^{(m)},
$$
and the subsequent parameter update divides the tokenwise modulation–input product $\sum_{g=1}^G R_i^{(m, g)} Z_{i, :}^{(m, g)}$ by $N = BT$ (`-(mod_flat.t() @ x_flat) / BT`). Ignoring the descent sign, the **implemented contraction in code** is therefore
$$
\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}}(G; \text{scale\_factor}) := \frac{1}{N} \sum_{g=1}^G R_i^{(m, g)}(\text{scale\_factor}) \, Z_{i, :}^{(m, g)} = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}}(G), \qquad \text{where} \qquad \widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}}(G) := \frac{1}{G} \sum_{g=1}^G R_i^{(m, g)}(1) \, Z_{i, :}^{(m, g)}.
$$
In particular, the default setting `scale_factor = 1` (`exp26`–`exp34`) computes $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}}(G; 1) = \frac{1}{M_g}\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}}(G)$, whereas setting `scale_factor = M_g` (`exp35`) computes $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}}(G; M_g) = \widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}}(G)$, and at $G = N$ ($M_g = 1$) both coincide identically.

Furthermore, we must distinguish **separable channels**—where coordinate $i$'s forward displacement depends only on $\xi_{n, i}$, as holds at **Stage 1** (`mlp.c_fc` with elementwise $\text{ReLU}^2$ and `attn.c_v` with linear head-value projection)—from **non-separable `RMSNorm` interfaces** at **Stage 2** (`x_final = RMSNorm(x_L)` and `x_0 = RMSNorm(e_tok)`), where $\text{rms}(z_n) = \sqrt{\frac{1}{D}\sum_{i'=1}^D z_{n, i'}^2}$ couples coordinate $i$ to $\xi_{n, i'}$ for $i' \neq i$.

**Proposition 1 (Conditional Moments of $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}}(G; \text{scale\_factor})$ for Separable Channels and `RMSNorm` Interfaces).** *Conditioned on fixed pre-synaptic inputs $\{x_n\}_{n=1}^N \subset \mathbb{R}^{D_{\text{in}}}$ and downstream weights/modulations independent of the i.i.d. Rademacher draws $\xi_{n, i} \sim \text{Unif}(\{\pm 1\})$:*
1. **(a) Separable-Channel Conditional Mean (Stage 1: `mlp.c_fc, attn.c_v`):** *Suppose $\Delta y_{n, i}^{(m)} = \sigma c_{n, i} \xi_{n, i} + r_{n, i}(\sigma)$ with $c_{n, i}$ independent of $\xi_n$ and $r_{n, i}(\sigma) = O(\sigma^2)$, and let $a_{n, i} := c_{n, i} \sum_{j=1}^{d_{\text{next}}} W_{j, i}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}}) u_{n, j}^{(m+1)}$. Then the first-order code estimator $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}, (1)}(G; \text{scale\_factor})$ satisfies for every $G \in \{1, \dots, N\}$ ($M_g = N / G$):*
   $$
   \mathbb{E}_{\xi}\left[\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}, (1)}(G; \text{scale\_factor})\right] = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \left(\frac{1}{N} \sum_{n=1}^N a_{n, i} x_n\right).
   $$
   *Specifically, under `scale_factor = 1`, the conditional mean scales as $\frac{1}{M_g}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^N a_{n, i} x_n\right)$ (evaluating to $0.5$ on $N=4, G=2, M_g=2, a_n = x_n = 1$), whereas under `scale_factor = M_g`, the conditional mean is $\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}, (1)}(G)] = \frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^N a_{n, i} x_n$ for all $G \in \{1, \dots, N\}$ (evaluating to $1.0$ on $N=4, G=2, M_g=2, a_n = x_n = 1$).*
2. **(b) Separable-Channel Symmetrized Pair Covariance ([5], Section 2.3):** *Under the separable channel model of (a), the exact conditional covariance matrix of $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}, (1)}(G; \text{scale\_factor})$ is*
   $$
   \text{Cov}_{\xi}\left(\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{code}, (1)}(G; \text{scale\_factor})\right) = \left(\frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g}\right)^2 \frac{1}{N^2} \sum_{g=1}^G \sum_{\substack{n, n' \in \mathcal{S}_g \\ n < n'}} \left(a_{n', i} x_n + a_{n, i} x_{n'}\right)\left(a_{n', i} x_n + a_{n, i} x_{n'}\right)^T,
   $$
   *which sums over the $G\binom{M_g}{2} = \frac{N(M_g - 1)}{2}$ unordered within-stream token pairs and vanishes identically at $G = N$ ($M_g = 1$).*
3. **(c) Non-Separable `RMSNorm` Interface Moments at $M_g = 1$ (Stage 2: `x_final, x_0`):** *At an `RMSNorm` interface $x_n = \text{RMSNorm}(z_n) = z_n / \text{rms}(z_n)$ ($z_n \in \mathbb{R}^D$), tangent-projected or raw Rademacher perturbation yields the first-order forward displacement $\Delta x_n^{(1)} = \frac{\sigma}{\text{rms}(z_n)} P_{x_n} \xi_n$, whose $i$-th coordinate is $\Delta x_{n, i}^{(1)} = \frac{\sigma}{\text{rms}(z_n)}\left((1 - x_{n, i}^2/D)\xi_{n, i} - \frac{x_{n, i}}{D}\sum_{i' \neq i} x_{n, i'}\xi_{n, i'}\right)$. Consequently, even at $G = N$ ($M_g = 1$, where cross-token pairs $n < n'$ are absent), for a single token $n$ with receiver sensitivity $a_{n, i} = \sum_j W_{j, i}^{(m+1)} u_{n, j}^{(m+1)}$ and pre-synaptic input $h_n \in \mathbb{R}^{D_{\text{in}}}$, the first-order unprojected coordinate-$i$ estimator is*
   $$
   \hat{g}_{n, i}^{(1)} := a_{n, i} \frac{\Delta x_{n, i}^{(1)} \xi_{n, i}}{\sigma} h_n = \frac{a_{n, i}}{\text{rms}(z_n)} \left(1 - \frac{x_{n, i}^2}{D} - \frac{x_{n, i}}{D} \sum_{i' \neq i} x_{n, i'} \xi_{n, i} \xi_{n, i'}\right) h_n,
   $$
   *which has exact conditional mean and nonzero single-token **cross-coordinate** covariance*
   $$
   \mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}\left[\hat{g}_{n, i}^{(1)}\right] = \frac{a_{n, i}}{\text{rms}(z_n)}\left(1 - \frac{x_{n, i}^2}{D}\right) h_n, \qquad \text{Cov}_{\xi_n}\left(\hat{g}_{n, i}^{(1)}\right) = \frac{a_{n, i}^2 x_{n, i}^2 (D - x_{n, i}^2)}{D^2 \, \text{rms}(z_n)^2} h_n h_n^T.
   $$
   *For example, for a single token ($N=G=M_g=1$) at $D=2$ with $z_1 = (1, 2)$, $a_{1, 1} = 1$, and $h_1 = 1$, we have $\text{rms}(z_1) = \sqrt{2.5}$, $x_{1, 1}^2 = 0.4$, $x_{1, 1} x_{1, 2} = 0.8$, and $\hat{g}_{1, 1}^{(1)} = \frac{0.8 - 0.4 \xi_{1, 1}\xi_{1, 2}}{\sqrt{2.5}}$, with mean $0.8/\sqrt{2.5} \approx 0.5060$ and variance $\frac{0.4(2 - 0.4)}{4 \times 2.5} = 0.064$. Moreover, applying reward-side radial projection $P_{x_n} = I_D - x_n x_n^\top / D$ to the vector of coordinatewise WM modulations $\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)} := (\Delta x_n^{(1)} \odot \xi_n \odot a_n)/\sigma$ yields the first-order modulation conditional expectation $\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}] = \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)} P_{x_n}\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_{n, i}^2/D) a_n$ and outer-product matrix expectation $\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{g}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}] = \mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}] h_n^\top$, which generally differ from the exact terminal-skip `RMSNorm` modulation $\mu_{\text{skip}, n} = \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)} P_{x_n} a_n$ and matrix pullback $g_{\text{skip}, n} = \mu_{\text{skip}, n} h_n^\top$: the modulation-vector discrepancy satisfies $\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}] - \mu_{\text{skip}, n}\right\|_2 = \left\|P_{x_n}\operatorname{diag}(x_{n, i}^2/D)a_n\right\|_2 / \text{rms}(z_n) \le \left(\frac{\max_i x_{n, i}^2}{D}\right) \frac{\|a_n\|_2}{\text{rms}(z_n)}$ and the matrix Frobenius discrepancy satisfies $\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{g}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}] - g_{\text{skip}, n}\right\|_F \le \left(\frac{\max_i x_{n, i}^2}{D}\right) \frac{\|a_n\|_2 \|h_n\|_2}{\text{rms}(z_n)}$ (where the normalized discrepancy coefficient $\max_i x_{n, i}^2 / D$ is $O(1/D)$ only under a coordinate-concentration assumption $\max_i x_{n, i}^2 = O(1)$, while the absolute discrepancy also depends on the scale factors $\|a_n\|_2, \|h_n\|_2, \text{rms}(z_n)$): for instance, at $D=3$ with unit-RMS $x = (\sqrt{3/2}, \sqrt{3/2}, 0)^\top$, $a = (1, 0, 1)^\top$, and $h = 1$, exact enumeration over all $2^3 = 8$ Rademacher patterns gives $\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[P_x \hat{g}_{\text{WM}}^{(1)}] = (1/4, -1/4, 1)^\top \neq g_{\text{skip}} = (1/2, -1/2, 1)^\top$ ($\cos = \frac{5}{3\sqrt{3}} \approx 0.962250$, `exp58`).*

*Proof.* For (a–b), expanding $\widehat{G}_{i, :}^{\text{norm}, (1)}(G) = \frac{1}{N}\sum_{g=1}^G (\sum_{n' \in \mathcal{S}_g} a_{n', i}\xi_{n', i})(\sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} \xi_{n, i} x_n)$ using $\xi_{n, i}^2 \equiv 1$ gives $\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^N a_{n, i} x_n + \frac{1}{N}\sum_{g=1}^G \sum_{n < n' \in \mathcal{S}_g} \eta_{\{n, n'\}, i} (a_{n', i} x_n + a_{n, i} x_{n'})$ where $\eta_{\{n, n'\}, i} = \xi_{n, i}\xi_{n', i}$ are pairwise uncorrelated with zero mean and unit variance; multiplying by $\text{scale\_factor} / M_g$ scales the mean by $\text{scale\_factor} / M_g$ and the covariance by $(\text{scale\_factor} / M_g)^2$. For (c), since $\xi_{n, i}^2 = 1$, multiplying $(P_{x_n}\xi_n)_i = (1 - x_{n, i}^2/D)\xi_{n, i} - \frac{x_{n, i}}{D}\sum_{i' \neq i} x_{n, i'}\xi_{n, i'}$ by $\xi_{n, i}$ yields the displayed expression for $\hat{g}_{n, i}^{(1)}$. Because $\{\xi_{n, i}\xi_{n, i'}\}_{i' \neq i}$ are $D - 1$ pairwise uncorrelated zero-mean Rademacher products with unit variance and $\sum_{i' \neq i} x_{n, i'}^2 = \|x_n\|_2^2 - x_{n, i}^2 = D - x_{n, i}^2$, the mean and covariance follow immediately, and multiplying the stacked coordinate expectations $\frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)}\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_{n, i}^2/D)a_n$ by $P_{x_n}$ gives $\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[P_{x_n}\hat{g}_{\text{WM}, n}^{(1)}]$. $\square$

### 3.3 Forward Causal Attention-Filtered Value Eligibility Trace (`Proposition 2`)

In causal multi-head self-attention with $H$ heads of dimension $D_h = D / H$, head $h$'s output at batch $b$, query token $\tau \in \{0, \dots, T-1\}$ is $y_{\text{attn}}^{(h)}(b, \tau) = \sum_{s \le \tau} A_{b, h, \tau, s} v^{(h)}(b, s)$, where $v^{(h)}(b, s) = W_v^{(h)} x_{\text{attn\_in}}(b, s)$ ($W_v^{(h)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D_h \times D}$) and $A_{b, h, \tau, s}$ is the causal attention weight ($\sum_{s \le \tau} A_{b, h, \tau, s} = 1$, $A_{b, h, \tau, s} = 0$ for $s > \tau$).

**Proposition 2 (Exact Commutation of $W_v^{(h)}$ with Causal Attention).** *Because $W_v^{(h)} \in \mathbb{R}^{D_h \times D}$ is shared across sequence positions $s \in \{0, \dots, T-1\}$, the post-attention value aggregation at position $\tau$ satisfies the exact linear identity*
$$
y_{\text{attn}}^{(h)}(b, \tau) = \sum_{s \le \tau} A_{b, h, \tau, s} \left(W_v^{(h)} x_{\text{attn\_in}}(b, s)\right) = W_v^{(h)} \underbrace{\left(\sum_{s \le \tau} A_{b, h, \tau, s} x_{\text{attn\_in}}(b, s)\right)}_{\bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}(b, \tau) \in \mathbb{R}^D}.
$$
*Consequently, injecting exploration noise $\psi_v^{(h)}(b, \tau) \sim \text{Unif}(\{\pm 1\}^{D_h})$ at the post-attention value output $y_{\text{attn}}^{(h)}(b, \tau)$ and recording the **forward causal attention-filtered pre-synaptic trace** $Z_{v}^{(h, g)}(t-2) = \sum_{(b, \tau) \in \mathcal{S}_g} (\psi_v^{(h)}(b, \tau)/\sigma_1) \, \bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}(b, \tau)^T$ yields an exact single-position linear channel $y_{\text{attn}}^{(h)}(b, \tau) = W_v^{(h)} \bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}(b, \tau)$ at each output token $\tau$, eliminating causal cross-position perturbation mixing with zero reverse-time pooling.*

### 3.4 Diagonal Identity-Skip Surrogate & Post-Installation Credit Decomposition (`Propositions 3 & 4`)

**Proposition 3 (Algebraic Diagonal Skip-Routing Surrogate for `wte`).** *In the unrolled residual equation $x_L = \alpha_0 x_0 + \dots$, the direct path from $x_0 = \text{RMSNorm}(W_{\text{wte}}[x])$ to $x_L$ is the fixed diagonal scaling $W_{\text{skip}} = \alpha_0 I_D$. Treating $W_{\text{skip}} = \alpha_0 I_D$ as an algebraic surrogate receiver matrix with post-synaptic modulation $s_{\text{bus}}(n) = \text{mod\_bus\_base}(n) \in \mathbb{R}^D$ and pre-synaptic forward displacement $\Delta x_0(n) = \text{RMSNorm}(e_{\text{tok}}(n) + \sigma \tilde{\xi}_{\text{wte}}(n)) - x_0(n)$, the column-norm contraction reduces to the $O(BT \cdot D)$ elementwise contraction*
$$
R_{\text{wte}, i}^{\text{raw}, (g)}(\text{scale\_factor}) = \sum_{j=1}^D (\alpha_0 \delta_{j, i}) \left(\frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g}\sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} s_{\text{bus}, n, j} \, \Delta x_{0, n, i}\right) = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} \left(\alpha_0 s_{\text{bus}, n, i}\right) \Delta x_{0, n, i},
$$
*(followed in `compute_identity_skip_wm_rewards_fast` by stream-mean centering $R_{\text{wte}, i}^{(g)} = R_{\text{wte}, i}^{\text{raw}, (g)} - \frac{1}{G}\sum_{g'=1}^G R_{\text{wte}, i}^{\text{raw}, (g')}$), eliminating the second $O(G V D)$ `lm_head` contraction.*

Finally, we analyze **Strict Post-Installation Construction** (`Stage 2: (t-1, t [post-Stage3-step], t)`), where Stage 2 evaluates `compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast` at clock $t$ on $W_{\text{lm}}(t) = W_{\text{lm}}(t-1) + \delta W_{\text{lm}}(t-1)$ after Stage 3 has installed its batch-$t-1$ update. In our implementation (`custom_entrywise_adamw_step` and `row_isotropic_adamw_step`), at optimizer step $t_{\text{step}}$, the installed Stage-3 displacement is
$$
\delta W_{\text{lm}, j, i}(t-1) = -\eta_3 \lambda_{\text{wd}} W_{\text{lm}, j, i}(t-1) - \frac{\eta_3}{1 - \beta_1^{t_{\text{step}}}} \cdot d_{j, i} \left(\beta_1 m_{j, i}^{(t-2)} + (1 - \beta_1) g_{j, i}^{(t-1)}\right), \qquad d_{j, i} := \frac{1}{\sqrt{\hat{v}_{j, i}^{(t-1)}} + \epsilon},
$$
where $g_{j, i}^{(t-1)} = -\frac{1}{BT}\sum_{n'=1}^{BT} s_{\text{lm}, n', j} x_{\text{final}, n', i}$, $\hat{v}_{j, i}^{(t-1)} = v_{j, i}^{(t-1)} / (1 - \beta_2^{t_{\text{step}}})$ under `entrywise` moments (or row-averaged $d_{j, i} \equiv d_j = (\sqrt{\hat{v}_j^{(t-1)}} + \epsilon)^{-1}$ with $\hat{v}_j^{(t-1)} = \frac{1}{D}\sum_{i=1}^D \hat{v}_{j, i}^{(t-1)}$ under `row_iso`), and $\epsilon > 0$ is added *outside* the square root (`denom = sqrt(v_hat) + eps`). At $G = BT$ ($M_g = 1$), let $u_{\text{bus}, i}^{\text{pre}}(n) = R_{\text{bus}, i}^{\text{pre}}(n) (\xi_{n, i}/\sigma)$ and $u_{\text{bus}, i}^{\text{post}}(n) = R_{\text{bus}, i}^{\text{post}}(n) (\xi_{n, i}/\sigma)$ denote the unprojected Stage-2 credit before and after installing $\delta W_{\text{lm}}(t-1)$.

**Proposition 4 (Decomposition of Installed-Update Terminal Credit Shift, Data-Dependent Fourth-Moment Bound, and Preconditioning Regimes).** *Under Strict Post-Installation Construction at $G = BT$ ($M_g = 1$), the post-minus-pre unprojected credit shift $\delta u_{\text{bus}}(n) := u_{\text{bus}}^{\text{post}}(n) - u_{\text{bus}}^{\text{pre}}(n)$ decomposes into three additive components corresponding to decoupled weight decay, first-moment history $m^{(t-2)}$, and current-batch gradient $g^{(t-1)}$:*
$$
\delta u_{\text{bus}, i}(n) = -\eta_3 \lambda_{\text{wd}} u_{\text{bus}, i}^{\text{pre}}(n) + \delta u_{\text{mom}, i}(n) + \delta u_{\text{cur}, i}^{(n' = n)}(n) + \delta u_{\text{cur}, i}^{(n' \neq n)}(n),
$$
*where:*
1. **Same-Token ($n' = n$) `RMSNorm` Radial-vs-Oblique Split and Data-Dependent Fourth-Moment Bound:** *With tangent-projected forward carrier $\tilde{\xi}_n = P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} \xi_n$ and raw carrier multiplication $\xi_n / \sigma$, the first-order same-token ($n'=n$) term is $\delta u_{\text{cur}, i}^{(n'=n, 1)}(n) = \frac{\eta_3 (1-\beta_1)}{(1-\beta_1^{t_{\text{step}}}) BT} c_{n, i} \, b_i(n)$, where $c_{n, i} := \sum_{j=1}^V d_{j, i} s_{\text{lm}, n, j}^2$ and the vector $b(n)$ splits into an $O(1)$ radial vector $b_{\text{rad}}(n) \parallel x_{\text{final}}(n)$ and an oblique `RMSNorm`-Jacobian coupling term $b_{\text{oblique}}(n)$:*
   $$
   b(n) := \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)}\left(x_{\text{final}}(n) \odot \xi_n \odot (P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} \xi_n)\right) = \underbrace{\frac{1}{\text{rms}(z_n)} x_{\text{final}}(n)}_{b_{\text{rad}}(n) \parallel x_{\text{final}}(n)} - \underbrace{\frac{\langle \xi_n, x_{\text{final}}(n)\rangle}{D \, \text{rms}(z_n)} \left(x_{\text{final}}(n)^{\odot 2} \odot \xi_n\right)}_{b_{\text{oblique}}(n)}.
   $$
   *Under **row-isotropic preconditioning** ($d_{j, i} \equiv d_j$, so $c_{n, i} \equiv c_n$ is a scalar), reward-side projection $P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)}$ annihilates $c_n b_{\text{rad}}(n)$ identically ($P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} b_{\text{rad}}(n) = 0$) while leaving $P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} b_{\text{oblique}}(n) \neq 0$ (e.g., at $D=2, z_n=(1, 2), \xi_n=(1, 1)$, $b(n)=(0.16, -0.16)$, $b_{\text{rad}}(n)=(0.4, 0.8)$, and $P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} b(n) = (0.192, -0.096)$). Defining the normalized coordinate fourth moment $\kappa_4(x) := \frac{1}{D}\sum_{i=1}^D x_i^4 \in [1, D]$, the expected squared norm of $P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} b(n)$ relative to $\|b_{\text{rad}}(n)\|_2^2 = D / \text{rms}(z_n)^2$ satisfies the exact **data-dependent fourth-moment bound***
   $$
   \frac{\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}\left[\|P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)} b(n)\|_2^2\right]}{\|b_{\text{rad}}(n)\|_2^2} \le \frac{\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}\left[\|b_{\text{oblique}}(n)\|_2^2\right]}{\|b_{\text{rad}}(n)\|_2^2} = \frac{\sum_{i=1}^D x_{\text{final}, n, i}^4}{D^2} = \frac{\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}}(n))}{D}.
   $$
   *In particular: (i) if $\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}}(n)) \le C = O(1)$ is uniformly bounded, the residual ratio is $\le C/D = O(1/D)$ (on trained ClimbMix `d6` states at $D=384$, we measure token-mean $\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}}) = 2.9398$, giving bound $\kappa_4/D = 0.007656$ and empirical ratio $0.007458$); (ii) without a fourth-moment bound, $\|x\|_2^2 = D$ alone does **not** imply a vanishing residual—for the 2-sparse family $x = \sqrt{D/5}(1, 2, 0, \dots, 0)$ ($z = x$), $\kappa_4(x)/D = 0.68$ and $\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\|P_x b\|_2^2]}{\|b_{\text{rad}}\|_2^2} = 0.0576$ for every $D \ge 2$; and (iii) under **entrywise preconditioning**, coordinate variation in $c_{n, i} = \sum_j d_{j, i} s_{\text{lm}, n, j}^2$ breaks exact radial collinearity ($P_{x_{\text{final}}(n)}(c_n \odot b_{\text{rad}}(n)) \neq 0$) unless $\epsilon \gg \sqrt{\hat{v}_{j, i}^{(t-1)}}$, so increasing $\epsilon: 10^{-8} \to 10^{-4}$ is required to damp entrywise coordinate spread.*
2. **Cross-Token ($n' \neq n$) Current-Gradient Drift Bound:** *Since $d_{j, i} = (\sqrt{\hat{v}_{j, i}^{(t-1)}} + \epsilon)^{-1} \le \epsilon^{-1}$, the cross-token ($n' \neq n$) current-gradient contribution satisfies*
   $$
   \left|\delta u_{\text{cur}, i}^{(n' \neq n)}(n)\right| \le \frac{\eta_3 (1 - \beta_1)}{(1 - \beta_1^{t_{\text{step}}}) BT \, \epsilon} \sum_{n' \neq n} \left(\sum_{j=1}^V |s_{\text{lm}, n, j}| \, |s_{\text{lm}, n', j}|\right) |x_{\text{final}, n', i}| \, \left|\frac{\Delta x_{\text{final}, n, i}}{\sigma}\right|.
   $$
3. **Proposition 5 (First-Order Radial Invariance in Forward Normalization vs. Exact Fourth-Moment Radial Variance in Unprojected Reward Reconstruction):** *Let $u \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h}$ be a query or key head vector normalized to $\text{rms}(u) = 1$ ($\|u\|_2^2 = d_h$), let $P_u := I_{d_h} - \frac{1}{d_h} u u^\top$ be its orthogonal tangent projector ($P_u^2 = P_u, \|u\|_2^2 = d_h$), and let $g \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h}$ be a deterministic local tangent sensitivity satisfying $\langle g, u \rangle = 0$, all expressed in the coordinate frame where $\xi \in \{-1, +1\}^{d_h}$ has independent Rademacher signs (specifically, when Rademacher signs $\xi_{\text{pre}} \in \{-1, +1\}^{d_h}$ are drawn in the pre-rotary basis before an orthogonal rotary transformation $R_\theta \in O(d_h)$, since $R_\theta$ preserves inner products, Euclidean norms, $P_{R_\theta u_{\text{pre}}} = R_\theta P_{u_{\text{pre}}} R_\theta^\top$, and covariance traces, the fourth-moment expression below holds with $(u, g) = (u_{\text{pre}}, g_{\text{pre}}) = (R_\theta^\top u_{\text{post}}, R_\theta^\top g_{\text{post}})$ in the pre-rotary coordinate frame). Forward `RMSNorm` removes the radial component of $\xi$ to first order ($\text{norm}(u + \sigma \xi) - u = \sigma P_u \xi + O(\sigma^2)$), so the first-order scalar reward $R^{(1)} = \sigma \langle g, P_u \xi \rangle = \sigma \langle g, \xi \rangle$ is identical whether $\xi$ is tangent-projected in the forward pass or not. However, reconstructing the post-synaptic modulation vector with the **unprojected** perturbation $\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi) := (R^{(1)}/\sigma)\xi = \langle g, \xi \rangle \xi$ vs. the **reward-side tangent-projected** vector $\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}(\xi) := P_u \hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi) = \langle g, \xi \rangle P_u \xi$ yields identical expectations $\mathbb{E}[\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}] = \mathbb{E}[\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}] = g$ and exact total-variance reduction*
   $$
   \Delta(u, g) := \operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}\!\left(\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}\right) - \operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}\!\left(\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}\right) = \mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\|(I_{d_h} - P_u)\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi)\right\|_2^2\right] = \frac{1}{d_h}\mathbb{E}\!\left[(g^\top \xi)^2 (u^\top \xi)^2\right] = \frac{1}{d_h}\sum_{1 \le i < j \le d_h} (u_i g_j + u_j g_i)^2 = \|g\|_2^2 - \frac{2}{d_h}\sum_{i=1}^{d_h} u_i^2 g_i^2 \ge 0.
   $$
   *Proof:* Since $I_{d_h} - P_u = \frac{1}{d_h} u u^\top$ with $\|u\|_2^2 = d_h$ and $\mathbb{E}[\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}] = \mathbb{E}[\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}] = g$, Pythagorean decomposition gives $\Delta(u, g) = \mathbb{E}[\|\frac{1}{d_h}(u^\top \hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi)) u\|_2^2] = \frac{1}{d_h}\mathbb{E}[(g^\top \xi)^2 (u^\top \xi)^2]$. Expanding $(g^\top \xi)^2 (u^\top \xi)^2 = \sum_{i,j,k,l} g_i g_j u_k u_l \xi_i \xi_j \xi_k \xi_l$ and taking expectations over independent Rademacher signs ($\mathbb{E}[\xi_i^4] = 1$, $\mathbb{E}[\xi_i^2 \xi_j^2] = 1$ for $i \neq j$, and $0$ otherwise) yields $\mathbb{E}[(g^\top \xi)^2 (u^\top \xi)^2] = \sum_i g_i^2 u_i^2 + \sum_{i \neq j} g_i^2 u_j^2 + 2\sum_{i \neq j} g_i g_j u_i u_j = \|g\|_2^2 \|u\|_2^2 + 2(g^\top u)^2 - 2\sum_{i=1}^{d_h} u_i^2 g_i^2$. Substituting $\|u\|_2^2 = d_h$ and $g^\top u = 0$ gives $\Delta(u, g) = \|g\|_2^2 - \frac{2}{d_h}\sum_{i=1}^{d_h} u_i^2 g_i^2 = \frac{1}{d_h}\sum_{1 \le i < j \le d_h}(u_i g_j + u_j g_i)^2 \ge 0$. $\square$
   *In particular: (i) for $d_h = 2$, $u = (\sqrt{2}, 0)^\top$ ($\|u\|_2^2 = 2 = d_h$), and $g = (0, 1)^\top$, $\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}(\xi) \equiv (0, 1)^\top$ has **zero variance** ($\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}(\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}) = 0$) whereas $\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}(\xi) = (\xi_1 \xi_2, 1)^\top$ has radial variance $\Delta(u, g) = 1$ (and similarly for $d_h = 4, u = (2, 0, 0, 0)^\top, g = (0, 1, 0, 0)^\top$, where $\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}(\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}) = 3$, $\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}(\hat{g}^{\text{proj}}) = 2$, and $\Delta(u, g) = 1$); (ii) at the boundary state $d_h = 2, u = (1, 1)^\top, g = (1, -1)^\top$, $u_1 g_2 + u_2 g_1 = 0$ so every reconstructed draw is already tangent and $\Delta(u, g) = 0$; and (iii) for dense unit-RMS vectors with $u_i^2 \approx 1$, $\Delta(u, g) \approx (1 - \frac{2}{d_h})\|g\|_2^2$ out of $\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Cov}(\hat{g}^{\text{unproj}}) = (d_h - 1)\|g\|_2^2$, a relative radial noise fraction of $\frac{d_h - 2}{d_h(d_h - 1)} \approx \frac{1}{d_h}$. On initialized `d6` Layer 0 ($d_h = 64$, `exp49` Part 1), this radial component accounts for $1.53\%$ of single-draw modulation energy (`0.1438 -> 0.1447` $K=1$ weight cosine; `0.7340 -> 0.7365` $K=64$ cosine). Because $1/d_h \approx 1.56\%$ is a small fraction of total modulation energy, end-to-end 360-step training losses with and without reward-side tangent projection are empirically very close (`2.0598 ± 0.0005` vs. `2.0595 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB at $M=2$ in `exp49`, and `2.0649 ± 0.0006` vs. `2.0649 ± 0.0006` in `exp45`).*
4. **Proposition 6 (Closed-Form Parameter-Free Wiener-MMSE Key-Gather Filter vs. Discounted and Attention-Weighted Gathering):** *For a single causal attention head with attention weights $A_{\tau, s}$ ($\tau \ge s, \sum_{s' \le \tau} A_{\tau, s'} = 1$), perturbing keys $k_{s'} \mapsto k_{s'} + \sigma \tilde{\xi}_{k, s'}$ with independent tangent Rademacher probes $\tilde{\xi}_{k, s'} = P_{k_{s'}} \xi_{k, s'}$ produces the first-order scalar query-token reward $R_{\text{head}, \tau}^{(1)} = \sigma \sum_{s' \le \tau} A_{\tau, s'} \langle c_{\tau, s'}, \tilde{\xi}_{k, s'}\rangle$, where $c_{\tau, s'} := \langle g_\tau, v_{s'} - y_\tau\rangle \frac{1}{\sqrt{d_h}} P_{k_{s'}} q_\tau \in \mathbb{R}^{d_h}$ so that the exact local key sensitivity is $g_{k, s} = \sum_{\tau \ge s} J_{\tau s}^\top g_\tau = \sum_{\tau \ge s} A_{\tau, s} c_{\tau, s}$. For any causal query-to-key gather matrix $W_{\tau, s}$ ($\tau \ge s$) and $M \ge 1$ independent probe draws averaged at key $s$, the linear estimator $\widehat{g}_{k, s}^{(W, M)} := \frac{1}{M}\sum_{r=1}^M \left(\sum_{\tau \ge s} W_{\tau, s} \frac{R_{\text{head}, \tau}^{(1, r)}}{\sigma}\right)\tilde{\xi}_{k, s}^{(r)}$ has conditional mean*
   $$
   \mathbb{E}_{\xi}\!\left[\widehat{g}_{k, s}^{(W, M)}\right] = \sum_{\tau \ge s} W_{\tau, s} A_{\tau, s} c_{\tau, s}.
   $$
   *Consequently: (i) unweighted causal gathering ($W_{\tau, s} \equiv 1$) is conditionally unbiased ($W_{\tau, s} A_{\tau, s} = A_{\tau, s}$) but accumulates cross-key noise $\sum_{s' \le \tau, s' \neq s} A_{\tau, s'}^2 \|c_{\tau, s'}\|_2^2$ from queries $\tau$ that do not attend to $s$ ($A_{\tau, s} \approx 0$); (ii) naive attention-weighted (`Rake`) gathering ($W_{\tau, s} = A_{\tau, s}$) suppresses non-attending queries but weights each future query by $A_{\tau, s}^2$ in expectation, introducing a squaring attenuation bias $(1 - A_{\tau, s}) A_{\tau, s} c_{\tau, s}$ on moderate links; and (iii) under an isotropic prior $\mathbb{E}[\|c_{\tau, s'}\|_2^2] = \sigma_c^2$ ($\langle c_{\tau, s'}, c_{\tau, s''}\rangle = 0$ for $s' \neq s''$), the per-pair cross-key interference objective $\operatorname{MSE}_{\tau, s}^{\text{cross}}(W_{\tau, s}; M) := \sigma_c^2 \left[(W_{\tau, s} - 1)^2 A_{\tau, s}^2 + \frac{W_{\tau, s}^2}{M}\sum_{s' \le \tau, s' \neq s} A_{\tau, s'}^2\right] = \sigma_c^2 \left[(W_{\tau, s} - 1)^2 A_{\tau, s}^2 + \frac{W_{\tau, s}^2}{M}\left(\|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2 - A_{\tau, s}^2\right)\right]$ (which models cross-key superposition noise from $s' \neq s$; including the within-key $s'=s$ off-diagonal Rademacher coordinate variance $\frac{d_h-1}{d_h} A_{\tau, s}^2$ replaces $\|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2 - A_{\tau, s}^2$ by $\|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2 - \frac{1}{d_h} A_{\tau, s}^2$) has unique minimizer $\partial \operatorname{MSE}_{\tau, s}^{\text{cross}} / \partial W_{\tau, s} = 0$ given by the parameter-free **Wiener-MMSE Key-Gather filter***
   $$
   W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(M) = \frac{M A_{\tau, s}^2}{M A_{\tau, s}^2 + \sum_{s' \le \tau, s' \neq s} A_{\tau, s'}^2} = \frac{M A_{\tau, s}^2}{(M - 1) A_{\tau, s}^2 + \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2} \in [0, 1] \qquad \left(\text{or } \frac{M A_{\tau, s}^2}{(M - 1/d_h) A_{\tau, s}^2 + \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2} \text{ with within-key coordinate variance}\right).
   $$
   *In particular, $W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(M) = 1$ whenever key $s$ carries all of query $\tau$'s attention mass ($A_{\tau, s} = 1 \implies \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2 = 1$, eliminating the $A_{\tau, s}^2$ squaring bias of `Rake`), whereas $W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(M) \approx \frac{M A_{\tau, s}^2}{\|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2} \to 0$ whenever query $\tau$ attends elsewhere ($A_{\tau, s} \ll \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2$), suppressing cross-key interference without a tuned discount factor $\gamma_K$.*

### 3.5 Fused Register-Tiled Contraction, Optimizer Co-Design, and Node-Perturbation Controls

1. **Eliminating 3D Outer-Product Materialization via Register-Tiled Contraction (`_triton_wm_reward_mg1_kernel`):** Building on the SRAM-fused outgoing-column contraction concept introduced in [5] (version 5), notice that in the stream-partitioned WM reward (Section 3.2),
   $$
   R_i^{(m, g)}(\text{scale\_factor}) = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \sum_{j=1}^{D_{\text{out}}} W_{j, i}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}}) \sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} u_{n, j}^{(m+1)} \, \Delta y_{n, i}^{(m)} = \frac{\text{scale\_factor}}{M_g} \sum_{n \in \mathcal{S}_g} \Delta y_{n, i}^{(m)} \left(\sum_{j=1}^{D_{\text{out}}} u_{n, j}^{(m+1)} W_{j, i}^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}})\right),
   $$
   the 3D plasticity tensor $\Delta \tilde{W}^{(m+1, g)} \in \mathbb{R}^{G \times D_{\text{out}} \times D_{\text{in}}}$ never needs to be materialized in GPU HBM.
