In our fused Triton kernel (`_triton_wm_reward_mg1_kernel` with tile sizes `BLOCK_G=4, BLOCK_R=64, BLOCK_C=64` for $M_g=1$, and `_triton_wm_reward_mg_grouped_kernel` for $M_g > 1$), each thread block loads a `(BLOCK_R, BLOCK_C)` tile of $W_{\text{rec}}$ from L2 cache once for `BLOCK_G` streams and accumulates `R[g, c]` directly in registers with **`0.0 MB` temporary HBM allocation**, cutting the 13-matrix `d6` WM kernel suite **$38.4\times$ from `134.31 ms` to `3.50 ms/step`** at $G=2,048$ (and **$4.4\times$ from `4.51 ms` to `1.03 ms/step`** at $G=64$).
2. **Joint Co-Design of the Optimizer and Delayed WM Credit:** Adapting the `Pre-Polar Muon` optimizer foundation of [8] to delayed WM, we note that the downstream optimizer step installs $W^{(m+1)}(t_{\text{constr}})$—which acts as Stage $m$'s **credit reader**:
   - **Update Orthogonalization and Receiver Stable Rank (Descriptive Association):** On our Seed-46 Block-0 diagnostic audit (`run_geo_and_attn_steering_audit.py`), under `AdamW` (`adamw_hidden`), Stage-2 receiver matrices $W^{(2)}$ exhibit low stable rank (`mlp.c_proj` $\operatorname{srank}(W) := \|W\|_F^2 / \|W\|_2^2 = 2.75$, `attn.c_proj` $\operatorname{srank}(W) = 1.11$). `Pre-Polar Muon` [8] orthogonalizes each step's momentum update direction (which does not constrain the accumulated weight matrix $W$ to a Stiefel manifold); descriptively, this update orthogonalization is associated with a higher accumulated receiver stable rank on Block-0 `mlp.c_proj` ($127.24$ under `paper20_prepolar` and $141.33$ under `codesigned_prepolar`, $51.4\times$ higher than `adamw_hidden`) and `attn.c_proj` ($54.85$ and $52.72$, $47.5\times$ higher than `adamw_hidden`).
   - **Scale-Matched Sparse Embedding Velocity & Post-Installation Drift Annealing:** Because `wte` is initialized at $\sigma_{\text{init}} = 1.0$ before `RMSNorm(e_tok)` ($\|W_{\text{wte}}[v]\|_2 \approx \sqrt{D} = 19.6$), small embedding learning rates (`lr_wte = 3e-3` to `4e-3`) rotate normalized token directions slowly. Matching sparse embedding step size on `d6` ($BT = 2,048$) to $\sigma_{\text{init}} = 1.0$ (`lr_wte = 0.12, lr_ve = 0.06, betas = (0.80, 0.95)`) and linearly annealing learning rates (`lrm: 1.0 -> 0.2`) increases held-out validation attention-map shift $\|\mathbf{A}^{(l)}(360) - \mathbf{A}^{(l)}(0)\|_F / \|\mathbf{A}^{(l)}(0)\|_F$ (Seed 46) by **$9.82\times$ in Layer 0 (`0.0838 -> 0.8233`)** and **$1.42\times$ averaged across all 6 layers (`0.6046 -> 0.8587`)**.
3. **Author-Defined Node-Perturbation Comparators (`SR-LNP` and `SR-NP`):** We compare Delayed WM against two specified node-perturbation recipes on the matched 4-matrix parameter subset (neither comparator is a zero-same-step-relay delayed pipeline, and neither is claimed as a replica of published Dust [9]):
   - **Surrogate-Routed Local Node Perturbation (`SR-LNP`, `train_dust_d6_fast` in `run_exp37_38_wm_vs_dust.py`):** Uses the same terminal-skip residual factors ($\gamma_l$ for blocks and $\alpha_0$ for `wte`) as Delayed WM, but differs in credit timing (it constructs $\hat{g}_{\text{bus}}$ via $K \in \{4, 16, 64\}$ perturbation sub-passes and immediately uses $\hat{g}_{\text{bus}}$ on the same step for embedding and hidden-layer estimates, whereas Delayed WM uses 3-stage delayed packets), perturbation location, and attention-credit treatment (scoring value perturbations locally with $\gamma = 0$ rather than via the forward attention-filtered trace). Thus, `SR-LNP` vs. Delayed WM compares two complete forward-only recipes rather than isolating sibling variance alone.
   - **Suffix-Recomputed Node Perturbation (`SR-NP`, `train_faithful_dust_d6` in `run_exp42_compliant_fp32_and_faithful_dust.py`):** An author-defined suffix-recomputed comparator on the matched 4-matrix `d6` subset ($K_{\text{site}} = 4 \implies 76$ suffix-recomputed draws/step $+ 24$ attention-internal draws/step) implementing Gaussian perturbations $a_t \sim \mathcal{N}(0, I)$, draw-mean centering $c_s^{(i)} = \tilde{\ell}_s - \ell_s^{(i)}$, suffix recomputation from block $l+1$ onward (`_run_suffix_from_block`), per-head local attention-output alignment $c_{s, h}^{(i)} = -\langle \hat{g}_{\text{attn\_out}, s, h}, \Delta o_{s, h}^{(i)} \rangle$, and future-token reward accumulation $r_{t, h}^{(i)} = \sum_{s \ge t} \gamma^{s - t} c_{s, h}^{(i)}$ with $\gamma = 0.98$ (whose discounted accumulation is verified by the `1.47` unit test). In `train_faithful_dust_d6`, both projection writers (`attn.c_proj` and `mlp.c_proj`) use $\hat{g}_{\text{wr}}$ obtained by perturbing the block-output residual after the MLP (which for `attn.c_proj` omits its path through the same block's MLP, a path not restored by suffix recomputation from block $l+1$ onward), and `lm_head` receives an analytic local loss gradient rather than vocabulary-slab perturbations.

---

## 4. Experimental Results and Mechanistic Verification

![Six-panel summary of genuinely backward-free 3-stage delayed eligibility-trace Weight Maximization, fused Triton kernels, co-designed optimizer, and 6-matrix / selective upper-half attention routing across exp26–exp51](supplement/figure-1.png)

### 4.1 Experimental Setup, Arm-Classification Ledger, Attention Backend, and Timing Provenance

All experiments (`208` multi-seed experimental arms = `623` total runs across seeds $\{46, 47, 48\}$ in `exp18, exp21, exp26`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp61`: **`145` three-seed strictly delayed zero-relay Delayed-WM arms [`435` runs]**, **`3` three-seed same-clock compositional-relay diagnostic controls in `exp53`–`exp54` [`9` runs]**, **`13` three-seed node-perturbation arms [`11` `SR-LNP` + `2` `SR-NP` = `39` runs]**, **`4` head/lexical-only backward-free anchors [`11` runs: three 3-seed + one 2-seed]**, **`7` three-seed local-pullback diagnostic controls in `exp36` [`21` runs, `Rev. Matmuls = 1`]**, and **`36` three-seed backpropagation reference arms [`108` runs: `21` global BP + `5` block-skip surrogate BP + `10` sub-block direct-skip surrogate BP across `d6` 360/720-step, widths $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$, and Native `50.3M`]**) use the supplied `rustbpe` tokenizer (`vocab_size = 8,192`) and ClimbMix data stream on two Transformer regimes:
1. **ClimbMix `d6` ($L=6, D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}, H=D/64, D_h=64, V=8,192, B=8, T=256, BT=2,048$ tokens/step, $360$–$1,004$ steps $= 0.737\text{M}$–$2.056\text{M}$ tokens):** At our primary width $D=384$ ($H=6$ heads), total parameters $= 26,346,060$. The model has three value-embedding layers at zero-based blocks `1, 3, 5`, each with a value-gate matrix `ve_gate` of shape $6 \times 32 = 192$ weights (`576` `ve_gate` weights total), plus `12` residual-mixing scalars (`resid_lambdas, x0_lambdas`). Accordingly: `active_params = 24,576,000` ($24.58\text{M}$, `93.2815%`) in the 4-matrix configuration (`mlp.c_fc, mlp.c_proj, attn.c_v, attn.c_proj` active per block, `attn.c_q, attn.c_k` $= 1,769,472$, `576` `ve_gate` weights, and `12` residual scalars frozen); `25,460,736` ($25.46\text{M}$, `96.6396%`) when `attn.c_q` or `attn.c_k` is also active (`exp30`–`exp31`, `exp45`), or when $(W_Q, W_K)$ are active on half the blocks (`3` of `6` blocks in `exp50`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp54`, `exp56`, `exp59`); `25,165,824` (`95.5202%`) when $(W_Q, W_K)$ are active only on the 2 full-window blocks `(3, 5)` (`exp51`); `26,345,472` ($26.35\text{M}$, `99.9978%` of total parameters) when all 6 principal hidden projections are active across all 6 blocks (`exp45`, `exp47`, `exp49`, `exp50`, `exp53`, `exp56`–`exp60`, where the `588` frozen entries consist of the `576` `ve_gate` weights at zero-based blocks `1, 3, 5` plus the `12` residual-mixing scalars); and `26,346,048` (`99.99995%` of total parameters and `100%` of matrix weights, leaving only the `12` residual-mixing scalars frozen) when `ve_gate` is also updated via Delayed WM (`exp49`, `exp51`). In Section 4.7 (`exp39`, `exp56`), we also scale width across $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$ (`active_params` $\in \{14.55\text{M}, 24.58\text{M}, 36.70\text{M}, 67.11\text{M}\}$ for 4-matrix and $\{15.33\text{M}, 26.35\text{M}, 39.84\text{M}, 74.19\text{M}\}$ for 6-matrix). Disjoint evaluation splits (`evaluate_bpb_disjoint`) score `dev_tokens = 261,889` (`dev_bytes = 1,037,477`) and `valb_tokens = 262,009` (`valb_bytes = 1,050,500`), where $\text{CE (nats)} = \text{BPB} \cdot \ln(2) \cdot (\text{bytes}/\text{tokens})$ and $\text{PPL} = \exp(\text{CE})$.
2. **Native `50,332,176` ($L=8, D=512, H=4, D_h=128, V=8,192, B_{\text{eff}}=64, T=2,048, BT=131,072$ tokens/step, $100$ steps $= 13,107,200$ training tokens):** Total parameters $= 50,332,176$, with `active_params = 46,137,344` ($46.14\text{M}$, `91.67%`) in the 4-matrix configuration (`c_q, c_k` $= 4,194,304$, `512` `ve_gate` matrix weights, and `16` residual scalars frozen), `active_params = 48,234,496` ($48.23\text{M}$, `95.83%`) in the 5-matrix Spherical Active-$W_Q$ (`exp46`) and Upper-Half `(4, 5, 6, 7)` Active-$(W_Q, W_K)$ (`exp55`, `exp61`) configurations (where `2,097,152` `c_k` or lower-half `c_q, c_k` weights, `512` `ve_gate` weights, and `16` scalars are frozen), and `active_params = 50,331,648` ($50.33\text{M}$, `99.9990%` of total parameters) in the 6-matrix Decoupled Active-$(W_Q, W_K)$ configuration (`exp48`, where all 6 principal hidden matrix families are active and the `528` frozen entries consist of `512` `ve_gate` matrix weights across the 4 value-embedding layers plus `16` `resid_lambdas, x0_lambdas` scalars). To fit stream-partitioned eligibility-trace tensors in GPU memory, the backward-free Native `50.3M` runs accumulate `grad_accum_steps = 16` physical microbatches of `micro_batch_size = 4` at $G_{\text{micro}} \in \{256, 1024, 8192\}$ (`G_total` $\in \{4096, 16384, 131072\}$ streams/step, corresponding to $M_g \in \{32, 8, 1\}$ tokens/stream), departing from the supplied task's single-microbatch $B=64$, $499.65\text{M}$-token backpropagation recipe. Disjoint evaluation splits score `dev_tokens = 523,826` (`dev_bytes = 2,126,272`, $\text{bytes}/\text{token} = 4.059119$) and `valb_tokens = 523,715` (`valb_bytes = 2,129,527`). Because both `DEV` and `VAL-B` are evaluated at checkpoints across the ablation campaign, `VAL-B` serves as a second disjoint validation stream rather than an unopened holdout.
3. **Attention Backend and Timing Provenance (`model_and_data.py`, `benchmark_uncontended_timings.json`):** In `model_and_data.py`, the `fa3` handle wraps PyTorch's native fused `F.scaled_dot_product_attention(..., is_causal=True)`. In Tables 4–6, `Run GPU` reports the **measured 3-seed training-region GPU time (`total_train_gpu_s_mean`)** summed over forward and learning-rule CUDA intervals across all training steps—excluding data loading, host-to-device input transfer, and evaluation checkpoints—so matched-time comparisons match **measured training-region GPU time** rather than total elapsed wall-clock time or FLOPs. Furthermore, as shown in Figure 1E and Tables 5–6, the specialized 4-matrix trainer in `exp37`/`exp39` (`~18.7–18.8 ms/step`, giving `6.74s` at 360 steps, `9.41s` at 500 steps, and `13.57s` at 720 steps) has slightly higher per-step throughput than the general 6-matrix/multi-probe trainer in `exp47`/`exp49`–`exp60` (`~21.35–21.65 ms/step` when running 4-matrix controls, giving `8.20s` at 384 steps, `9.39s` at 438 steps, `15.62s` at 720 steps, `18.91s` at 875 steps, and `21.55s` at 1,004 steps) due to the general trainer's per-block probe-list and gate bookkeeping; we therefore plot their trajectories as separate trainer curves in Figure 1E and conduct all 4-vs-6-matrix matched-training-time comparisons (`384, 438, 720, 840, 875, 1,004` steps) strictly inside the same `exp49`–`exp60` trainer. `Est.` reports the **30-step steady-state benchmark estimate (`est_360step_s` in `benchmark_uncontended_timings.json`)**, which measures 35 steps on an uncontended RTX 4090, discards 5 warmup steps, and excludes evaluation, dataloader, and host-transfer overhead. In Tables 3 and 7, `Fwd / Rule / Wall (ms)` reports per-step forward GPU time, learning-rule GPU time, and either total step GPU time (`Fwd + Rule` in `exp40`–`exp48`, `exp55`, `exp61`) or complete elapsed wall-clock step time including evaluation checkpoints (`t_wall_step_ms_including_eval` in `exp26`–`exp36`).

### 4.2 Verification of Propositions 1 and 2: Stream Partitioning, Normalization Controls, and Filtered Traces (`exp26`, `exp28`, `exp35`)

Table 1 reports the single-step cosine similarity at initialization between our backward-free eligibility-trace pseudo-gradient estimator and the **language-model cross-entropy loss gradient** $\nabla_W \mathcal{L}_{\text{CE}}$ (computed via a separate diagnostic reference call `loss_ref.backward()`) on `d6` ($BT = 2,048$) across stream counts $G = BT / M_g$ (`exp26_results.json`, `exp28_results.json`). We emphasize that Table 1 is a single-step directional diagnostic rather than a claim of maintained gradient alignment throughout training.

| Stream Count $G$ ($M_g = BT / G$) | Stage 2 `wte` Cos | Stage 2 `mlp.c_proj` Cos | Stage 2 `attn.c_proj` Cos | Stage 1 `mlp.c_fc` Cos | Stage 1 `attn.c_v` (Unfiltered $x_{\text{in}}$) | Stage 1 `attn.c_v` (Filtered $\bar{x}_{\text{in}}$, Prop. 2) |
| :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| $G = 1$ ($M_g = 2,048$) | $+0.0313$ | $+0.0187$ | $+0.0170$ | $+0.0037$ | $+0.0036$ | $+0.0295$ |
| $G = 64$ ($M_g = 32$) | $+0.2034$ | $+0.2978$ | $+0.2469$ | $+0.0305$ | $+0.0222$ | $+0.2361$ |
| $G = 256$ ($M_g = 8$) | $+0.3941$ | $+0.5341$ | $+0.4570$ | $+0.1430$ | $+0.0416$ | $+0.4519$ |
| **$G = 2,048$ ($M_g = 1$)** | **$+0.9139$** | **$+0.9364$** | **$+0.9068$** | **$+0.9089$** | $+0.1104$ | **$+0.9069$ ($8.2\times$)** |

**Table 1:** Single-step cosine alignment with the language-model cross-entropy loss gradient $\nabla_W \mathcal{L}_{\text{CE}}$ on ClimbMix `d6` ($BT = 2,048$) across stream counts $G \in \{1, 64, 256, 2048\}$ (`exp26`, `exp28`).

**Stream Scale-Normalization Controls (`exp35` Part A):** To test whether the stream scaling effect in Table 1 survives when update magnitude is held invariant to $M_g$ (Proposition 1), we ran a controlled 3-seed comparison on `d6` setting `scale_factor = float(M_g)` so that $\widehat{G}_{\text{norm}}(G) = M_g \widehat{G}_{\text{code}}(G)$ has the identical conditional mean $\frac{1}{N}\sum_n a_{n, i} x_n$ across all stream sizes:
- At $G = 1$ ($M_g = 2,048$): `scale_factor = 2048.0` yields **`2.6097 ± 0.0111` DEV BPB** (`2.5719 ± 0.0106` VAL-B), compared to `scale_factor = 1.0` (`2.5939 ± 0.0109` DEV BPB).
- At $G = 64$ ($M_g = 32$): `scale_factor = 32.0` yields **`2.3361 ± 0.0084` DEV BPB** (`2.2995 ± 0.0086` VAL-B), compared to `scale_factor = 1.0` (`2.3364 ± 0.0051` DEV BPB).
- At $G = 256$ ($M_g = 8$): `scale_factor = 8.0` yields **`2.3008 ± 0.0074` DEV BPB** (`2.2635 ± 0.0065` VAL-B), compared to `scale_factor = 1.0` (`2.2966 ± 0.0115` DEV BPB).
- On the canonical `Pre-Polar Muon` trainer (`strict_post_install`, `entrywise_b09_eps1e4`, $G=64$, `exp35` Part A2): `scale_factor = 1.0` reaches **`2.2541 ± 0.0013` DEV BPB** and `scale_factor = 32.0` reaches **`2.2539 ± 0.0013` DEV BPB** (agreeing to within $0.0002$ BPB).
These controls establish that the learning gains from increasing $G$ ($M_g \to 1$) are driven by the elimination of within-stream cross-token pair interference (Proposition 1(b)), not by artificial update rescaling.

### 4.3 Verification of Propositions 3 and 4: 20-Step Post-Installation Credit Drift, Kurtosis Audit, and Batch-Size Occupancy (`exp31`, `exp34`–`exp36`)

To test how receiver weight installation affects Stage-1 (`c_proj` receiver) and Stage-2 (`lm_head` receiver) rewards, we measured the 20-step mean cosine similarity, relative L2 norm drift $\|u_{\text{post}} - u_{\text{pre}}\|_2 / \|u_{\text{pre}}\|_2$, activation fourth moments, and batch-size occupancy on `d6` (`exp31`, `exp34`–`exp36` Part 0, Table 2):

1. **Stage-1 Receiver Drift (`exp31` Part 0, `exp35` Part 0):** When Stage 2 (`mlp.c_proj, attn.c_proj`) is updated with `AdamW`, one receiver step $\delta W^{(2)}$ induces a **`23.56%` relative L2 drift** in Stage-1 rewards ($\cos = 0.976474$). Replacing `AdamW` on Stage 2 with Stiefel-orthogonalized `Pre-Polar Muon` reduces Stage-1 relative L2 reward drift to **`2.85%` ($\cos = 0.999597$)** in `exp31` and **`4.04%` ($\cos = 0.999238$)** in `exp35` Part 0.
2. **Activation Kurtosis Expectation Bound vs. Realized Token Ratios (`exp35` Part 0):** Across the 20-step diagnostic trajectory (`run_part0_kurtosis_and_rmsnorm_variance_audit`, which updates `lm_head` and block-0 `mlp.c_proj` over 20 steps on `d6`), the normalized fourth moment $\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}}) = \frac{1}{D}\sum_{i=1}^D x_{\text{final}, i}^4$ has token mean **`2.9398`** (token max `4.2932`). Proposition 4 bounds the **expected** first-order same-token residual ratio $\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\xi_n}[\|P_x b\|_2^2]}{\|b_{\text{rad}}\|_2^2} \le \frac{\kappa_4(x_{\text{final}})}{D}$; on these states, the mean theoretical expectation bound is **`0.007656`** and the empirical mean ratio across all $40,960$ token evaluations is **`0.007458`**, while individual realized draws $\xi_n$ can fluctuate higher (token maximum **`0.124439`**).
3. **Stage-2 Strict Post-Install Terminal Drift Across the $2 \times 2 \times 2$ Optimizer Cube (`exp34` Part 0, Table 2) and Batch-Size Occupancy (`exp36` Part 0A):** Across all 8 cells of `{entrywise, row_iso} x {beta1=0.5, 0.9} x {eps=1e-8, 1e-4}`, combining $(\beta_1=0.9, \epsilon=10^{-4})$ cuts current-gradient drift by **$47.8\%$ (`0.3511 -> 0.1832`)** and total post-install drift from `39.41%` to **`26.05%` ($\cos = 0.964738$)**. Furthermore, holding the `d6` architecture and sequence length $T=256$ fixed while sweeping batch size $BT \in \{512, 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192\}$ (`exp36` Part 0A) shows that singleton target-token occupancy decreases monotonically (`0.3957 -> 0.3516 -> 0.2958 -> 0.2265 -> 0.1620`) and post-installation relative L2 credit drift decreases monotonically (**`44.88% -> 41.95% -> 39.41% -> 37.46% -> 35.68%`**), confirming that larger effective token batches dilute single-token self-update collisions in $\delta W_{\text{lm}}(t-1)$.

**Table 3:** Controlled 3-seed ClimbMix `d6` progression, complete 16-cell $2 \times 2 \times 2 \times 2$ Stage-2 Clock $\times$ Optimizer cube (`exp34` + `exp35` Part B1), 4-cell True Both-Stages Pre-Install comparison (`exp35` Part B2), compliant zero-pullback pure-`FP32` trace-cache clock controls (`exp42` Part A), local-pullback `s_lm @ W_lm` clock controls (`exp36` Part A, `Rev. Matmuls = 1`), and realized-component reader controls (`exp36` Part B).

**Key Mechanistic Findings:**
1. **Stage-1 vs. Stage-2 Post-Installation Drift Separation and Compliant Zero-Pullback Pure-`FP32` Clock Controls (`exp35` Part B2, `exp42` Part A):** Comparing True Both-Stages Pre-Install `(t-1, t-1, t)/(t-2, t-1, t)` with Stage-2 Pre-Install `(t-1, t-1, t)/(t-2, t, t)` shows that delaying Stage-1 reward construction until step $t$ shifts DEV BPB by only **`+0.0013` to `+0.0024` BPB** across all 4 `entrywise` cells (`2.1341 -> 2.1360` at `b05, 1e-8`; `2.1195 -> 2.1217` at `b05, 1e-4`), whereas delaying Stage-2 reward construction until step $t$ (`strict_post_install`) shifts DEV BPB by **`+0.0219 ± 0.0020` BPB** at `(entrywise, b05, 1e-8)` (`2.1360 -> 2.1579`). Because `exp34` cached `mod_bus_base` in `BF16` on the pre-install path and cached `x_final_clean` in `BF16` on the post-install path (`zero_dw_bf16_cache_rel_l2_diff_mean = 0.001687`), and because `exp36` Part A used a local pullback `s_lm @ W_lm` (`Rev. Matmuls = 1`), `exp42` Part A (`exp42_fp32_triton_results.json`) executes a strictly zero-pullback (`reverse_error_matmuls_per_step = 0`) pure-`FP32` trace-cache control via `_triton_wm_reward_mg1_kernel` (`zero_dw_fp32_cache_rel_l2_diff_mean = 0.0`). Across 3 seeds (`46, 47, 48`), the compliant zero-pullback pure-`FP32` clock contrast is **`2.1359 ± 0.0022` vs. `2.1579 ± 0.0004` DEV BPB (`+0.0219 ± 0.0019` BPB)** at `b05, 1e-8` and **`2.1274 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB (`2.0901 ± 0.0001` VAL-B BPB)** at `b09, 1e-4`, confirming that the clock shift is a genuine receiver-installation effect.
2. **Row-Isotropic vs. Entrywise Preconditioning and Realized-Component Readers (`Proposition 4`, `exp34`–`exp36`):** Under `row_iso` preconditioning, Pre-Install and Strict Post-Install agree to within $\le 0.0003$ BPB across all 4 cells (`2.1577` vs. `2.1580`; `2.1662` vs. `2.1659`; `2.1296` vs. `2.1298`; `2.1335` vs. `2.1334`), consistent with Proposition 4's radial cancellation under scalar $c_n$. Under `entrywise` preconditioning, increasing $\epsilon: 10^{-8} \to 10^{-4}$ improves Strict Post-Install by **`-0.0279 ± 0.0001` BPB** (`2.1579 -> 2.1300`), and combining $(\beta_1=0.9, \epsilon=10^{-4})$ reaches **`2.1273 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB** (`2.1274 ± 0.0002` in pure `FP32`). Conversely, folding the internal `Pre-Polar Muon` operator (`2.1613` vs. `2.1579` at `b05, 1e-8`; `2.1338` vs. `2.1273` at `b09, 1e-4`, `exp36` Part B) or the `lm_head` `AdamW` preconditioner (`2.2128` vs. `2.1579`) directly into the WM reader degrades learning because forward perturbations propagate through the unpreconditioned forward weights $W_{\text{live}}$.

### 4.5 Fused Register-Tiled Triton WM Speedup and Node-Perturbation Comparator Timing (`exp37`, `exp42`)

Profiling `compute_stream_wm_rewards_fast` in `exp34` revealed that materializing the 3D intermediate outer-product tensor $\Delta \tilde{W}^{(m+1, g)} \in \mathbb{R}^{G \times D_{\text{out}} \times D_{\text{in}}}$ in GPU HBM (`3,498.9 MB/step` across 13 matrices on `d6`) consumed **`134.31 ms/step`** (`86.3%` of total step time). Table 4 compares our fused register-tiled Triton kernels (`_triton_wm_reward_mg1_kernel` for $M_g=1$ and `_triton_wm_reward_mg_grouped_kernel` for $M_g > 1$), batched `Pre-Polar Muon` (`prepolar_muon_orthogonalize_batched`: `10.35 -> 1.37 ms/step`), **Surrogate-Routed Local Node Perturbation (`SR-LNP`)** (`train_dust_d6_fast`, using `@torch.compile` fused TensorCore chunk kernels `fused_bus_ce_chunk`, `fused_wte_dust_chunk`, `fused_mlp_dust_chunk`), and **Suffix-Recomputed Node Perturbation (`SR-NP`)** (`train_faithful_dust_d6` in `run_exp42_compliant_fp32_and_faithful_dust.py`, which executes exact suffix recomputation from block $l$ onward across $K_{\text{tot}} = 76$ block-level draws plus $24$ local attention draws per step with $\gamma = 0.98$) on an uncontended RTX 4090 (`benchmark_uncontended_timings.json`, `exp37_39_master_results.json`, `exp42_faithful_dust_results.json`). Both 30-step steady-state benchmark estimates (`est_360step_s`) and actual 3-seed training GPU times (`total_train_gpu_s_mean`) are reported.

### 4.6 Joint Co-Design of the Optimizer, Three-Tier Attention Hierarchy, Full 6-Matrix + Exploratory `ve_gate` Routing, `Wiener-MMSE` / `Hadamard-4` / Sparse `Top-K=32` Key Gather, and Same-Trainer Matched-Training-Time Controls (`exp38`, `exp45`, `exp47`, `exp49`–`exp51`, `exp59`, `exp60`)

Table 5 reports our controlled 3-seed optimizer co-design, 5/6-matrix attention-routing, exploratory active-`ve_gate`, $2 \times 2$ block-depth factorial, closed-form `Wiener-MMSE` / `Hadamard-4` / sparse `Top-K=32` / Stage-2 leverage-inversion controls, and same-trainer matched-training-region-time ablations on ClimbMix `d6` ($360$–$1,004$ steps, Strict Post-Installation Construction `(t-1, t [post-Stage3], t)/(t-2, t [post-Stage2], t)`, `exp37_39_master_results.json`, `exp45_d6_attn_routing_results.json`, `exp47_d6_6mat_joint_qk_results.json`, `exp49_d6_equal_compute_and_ve_gate_results.json`, `exp50_d6_equal_compute_qk_schedules_results.json`, `exp51_d6_720step_scaling_and_bp_controls_results.json`, `exp59_rake_key_gather_and_clock_controls_results.json`, `exp60_hadamard_qk_multiplexing_and_leverage_inversion_results.json`) alongside the Seed-46 Block-0 geometric diagnostic (`run_geo_and_attn_steering_audit.py`, `geo_and_attn_steering_audit.json`):
1. **Orthogonalized Update Directions and Receiver Stable Rank (`51.4x` Descriptive Association on Seed 46 Block 0):** Measuring Block 0 at step 360 on Seed 46 (`run_geo_and_attn_steering_audit.py`), under `AdamW` on hidden matrices (`adamw_hidden`), Stage-2 receiver columns collapse onto a low-rank subspace (`mlp.c_proj` stable rank $\operatorname{srank}(W) = 2.75$, off-diagonal column coherence $0.3829$; `attn.c_proj` $\operatorname{srank}(W) = 1.11$, coherence $0.6024$). Under `Pre-Polar Muon`, orthogonalizing each step's momentum update direction is descriptively associated with a higher Block-0 weight stable rank on `mlp.c_proj` (**`127.24` under `paper20_prepolar`, coherence $0.0419$; and `141.33` under `codesigned_prepolar`, coherence $0.0411$, $51.4\times$ higher than `adamw_hidden`**) and `attn.c_proj` (**`54.85` under `paper20_prepolar` and `52.72` under `codesigned_prepolar`, $47.5\times$ higher than `adamw_hidden`**).
2. **Scale-Matched Sparse Embedding Velocity (`9.82x` Layer-0 / `1.42x` 6-Layer Mean Attention Shift) & Drift Annealing:** Matching sparse embedding angular velocity (`lr_wte = 0.12, lr_ve = 0.06, betas = (0.80, 0.95)`) to $\sigma_{\text{init}}(\text{wte}) = 1.0$ before `RMSNorm(e_tok)` (`-0.0194` BPB gain alone: `2.1273 -> 2.1079`) and linearly annealing learning rates (`lrm: 1.0 -> 0.5`: `-0.0296` BPB to `2.0783`; `lrm: 1.0 -> 0.2`: `-0.0101` BPB to `2.0682`) improves 3-seed 360-step `d6` validation BPB from `2.1273 ± 0.0001` to **`2.0682 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`2.0315 ± 0.0004` VAL-B BPB, `292.66` PPL)**—within **`0.0150` BPB** of matched-subset Exact Backpropagation (`2.0532 ± 0.0008`). On the first held-out validation batch (`split="val"`, `B=8, T=256`, Seed 46), the co-designed schedule increases causal attention-map relative Frobenius shift $\|\mathbf{A}^{(l)}(360) - \mathbf{A}^{(l)}(0)\|_F / \|\mathbf{A}^{(l)}(0)\|_F$ by **$9.82\times$ in Layer 0 (`0.0838 -> 0.8233`)** and **$1.42\times$ averaged across all 6 layers (`0.6046 -> 0.8587`)** (`geo_and_attn_steering_audit.json`: per-layer shifts `[0.0838, 0.5327, 0.6784, 0.7494, 0.7733, 0.8100] -> [0.8233, 0.8472, 0.8682, 0.8639, 0.8690, 0.8807]`) even with $W_Q, W_K$ frozen. Notably, matched-subset Exact Backpropagation also improves by **`-0.0548` BPB (`2.1080 ± 0.0006 -> 2.0532 ± 0.0008`)** under the same optimizer package, whereas applying the same package to `SR-LNP (K=16)` (`dust_K16_matched_codesigned_opt`) yields **`2.2602 ± 0.0010` vs. `2.2603 ± 0.0018` DEV BPB** (`-0.0001` BPB), establishing an empirical interaction between the optimizer package and the credit estimator in that `SR-LNP` configuration.
3. **Three-Tier Attention Credit-Assignment Hierarchy ($W_V$ vs. $W_Q$ vs. $W_K$), Key Truncation vs. Future-Query Accumulation, and 5-Matrix Spherical Active-$W_Q$ (`exp45`, `exp47` Part 1, `exp49` Part 1):** Building on the per-head attention-output reward, separate attention-internal jittering (including gates), token-local query credit ($\gamma = 0$), and discounted future-token key credit ($\gamma = 0.98$) formulations of Dust [9] (`Section 2.3`) and concurrent `general_pretraining_18/v9`–`v10` [5], extending our backward-free Stage-1 estimator to `attn.c_q` ($W_Q$) and `attn.c_k` ($W_K$) reveals a three-tier hierarchy in single-draw ($K=1$) credit assignment at $M_g = 1$, governed by the effective coupled subspace dimension $m_{\text{eff}}$. In our diagnostic suite (`run_attention_subspace_cosine_diagnostic`), pre-attention activations are held fixed and downstream gradient $\nabla_{y_{\text{attn}}}\mathcal{L}$ is supplied as an oracle downstream vector (`gy_attn` from `loss.backward()` at initialized `seed = 46`, Layer 0) specifically to isolate single-block attention transport mechanics from multi-layer delayed credit variance:
   - **Tier 1 (`attn.c_v`, $m_{\text{eff}} = 1$, Coordinate-Isolated & Forward-Factorizable via $\bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}$):** By Proposition 2, perturbing $y_{\text{attn}}^{(h)}(b, \tau)$ coordinate-by-coordinate and contracting against $\bar{x}_{\text{attn\_in}}^{(h)}(b, \tau)$ isolates every head coordinate without cross-coordinate or cross-token coupling, satisfying an exact conditional single-block reconstruction identity **`cos = 1.0000`** given oracle downstream credit at $K=1$ (distinct from its `+0.9069` end-to-end delayed backward-free cosine in Table 1).
   - **Tier 2 (`attn.c_q`, $m_{\text{eff}} = d_h = 64$, Token-Local in Time $\tau$ / Coupled Across $d_h$ Head Channels):** Because query $q_\tau^{(h)}$ enters only row $\tau$ of the causal attention matrix $\mathbf{A}_{b, h, \tau, :}$, perturbing $q_\tau^{(h)} \mapsto q_\tau^{(h)} + \sigma \tilde{\xi}_{q, \tau}^{(h)}$ alters only output token $\tau$'s attention output $\Delta y_{\text{attn}}^{(h)}(b, \tau)$, so **zero cross-time interference arises at $M_g = 1$**. However, all $d_h = 64$ coordinates of $q_\tau^{(h)}$ mix into the scalar dot product $\langle q_\tau^{(h)}, k_s^{(h)}\rangle / \sqrt{d_h}$, sharing a single per-head reward $R_{q, \tau}^{(h)} = \sum_{c=1}^{d_h} R_{\text{attn\_proj}, \tau, (h, c)}$. For this specific single-draw per-head estimator at the initialized diagnostic state (`attention_subspace_cosine_diagnostic`), empirical cosine alignment is **`cos = 0.1357` at $K=1$** (comparable to $1 / \sqrt{d_h} = 0.1250$), scaling with draw count $K$ to **`0.2657` at $K=4$, `0.4748` at $K=16$, and `0.7385` at $K=64$**.
   - **Tier 3 (`attn.c_k`, Non-Local Across Future Queries $\tau \ge s$, Truncation Bias vs. Discounted Future-Query Credit, Closed-Form Wiener-MMSE Key Gather [`Proposition 6`, `exp47` Part 1, `exp59`], & Sparse Top-$K_{\text{echo}}=32$ Key Gather [`exp60`]):** Key $k_s^{(h)}$ enters the attention distribution of *every future query* $\tau \in \{s, \dots, T-1\}$ via the query-dependent softmax Jacobian $\operatorname{diag}(A_\tau) - A_\tau A_\tau^T$, which does not factorize into a query-independent forward trace. Conditioned on fixed pre-attention activations and downstream sensitivity vectors $\{g_\tau\}$, the discounted future-query key estimator satisfies the first-order expectation identity $\mathbb{E}[\widehat{g}_{k_s}^{(\gamma_K)}] = \sum_{\tau \ge s} \gamma_K^{\tau - s} J_{\tau s}^\top g_\tau + O(\sigma)$. Multiplying `r_head` at position $s$ by $\xi_{k,s}$ at the *same* position $s$ ($\gamma_K = 0$) captures only the diagonal self-attention sensitivity $J_{ss}^\top g_s$ while omitting all future-query credit $\sum_{\tau > s} J_{\tau s}^\top g_\tau$ (`cos = 0.0076` at $K=1$, `0.0534` at $K=64$). In our pure-Python 2-token finite-difference Rademacher enumeration (`eps_fd = 1e-5`, verified to tolerance `1e-9` in `verify_paper5.py`), same-position key correlation yields $(0.0, 0.0)$ across all 16 perturbation patterns when loss is supported at token 2, whereas accumulating future query rewards $\widetilde{R}_{k,s}^{(\gamma_K)} = \sum_{\tau \ge s, \tau - s \le W_l} \gamma_K^{\tau - s} R_{\text{head},\tau}$ at $\gamma_K = 1$ restores all future terms and matches the finite-difference key derivative $[0.317182, 0.422909]$. Across an 8-point discount horizon sweep $\gamma_K \in \{0.00, 0.50, 0.75, 0.85, 0.90, 0.95, 0.98, 1.00\}$ on `d6` Layer 0 (`exp47` Part 1), $K=64$ cosine alignment rises from **`0.0534` ($\gamma_K=0.00$) $\to$ `0.0887` ($0.50$) $\to$ `0.1171` ($0.75$) $\to$ `0.1329` ($0.85$) $\to$ `0.1423` ($0.90$) $\to$ `0.1529` ($0.95$) $\to$ `0.1565` ($0.98$)** before dipping to **`0.1340` ($\gamma_K=1.00$)**: $\gamma_K = 1$ removes truncation bias completely, while $\gamma_K < 1$ (e.g., $\gamma_K \in [0.85, 0.98]$) introduces a truncation bias whose magnitude depends on the omitted future sensitivity mass $\sum_{\tau > s} (1 - \gamma_K^{\tau - s}) J_{\tau s}^\top g_\tau$ in exchange for lower cross-time key superposition variance across the $T - s$ future query terms. Going beyond fixed recency discounting, **Proposition 6 (`exp59`)** shows that for a general causal gather matrix $W_{\tau, s}$ in $\widetilde{R}_{k, s}^{(W)} = \sum_{\tau \ge s} W_{\tau, s} R_{\text{head}, \tau}$, because the forward softmax perturbation $k_{s'} \mapsto k_{s'} + \sigma \tilde{\xi}_{k, s'}$ already multiplies key $s$'s contribution inside query $\tau$'s reward by $A_{\tau, s}$ ($\mathbb{E}[R_{\text{head}, \tau}\tilde{\xi}_{k, s}/\sigma] = A_{\tau, s} c_{\tau, s}$), naive attention-weighted (`Rake`) gathering $W_{\tau, s} = A_{\tau, s}$ weights each future query by $A_{\tau, s}^2$ in expectation, whereas the closed-form parameter-free **Wiener-MMSE Key-Gather filter** $W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(M) = \frac{M A_{\tau, s}^2}{(M - 1)A_{\tau, s}^2 + \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2} \in [0, 1]$ eliminates the $A_{\tau, s}^2$ squaring bias on dominant links ($A_{\tau, s} \to 1 \implies W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}} \to 1$) while suppressing cross-key noise when query $\tau$ attends elsewhere ($A_{\tau, s} \ll \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2 \implies W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}} \to 0$). On `d6` ($M=4$, `exp59` Part 1), moving from $\gamma_K=0.85$ $\to$ `rake_attn` ($W_{\tau, s}=A_{\tau, s}$) $\to$ `wiener_mmse` ($W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(4)$) increases 6-block mean key cosine alignment **`2.98x` at Step 0 (`0.0317 -> 0.0870 -> 0.0945`; Upper-Half `3..5`: `0.0338 -> 0.0870 -> 0.0935`)** and **`3.22x` at Step 120 after attention heads sharpen (`0.0284 -> 0.0727 -> 0.0914`, $+25.7\%$ over `rake_attn`; Upper-Half `3..5`: `0.0223 -> 0.0618 -> 0.0753`, `3.38x`)**. Moreover, because $W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(M)$ is rational quadratic in $A_{\tau, s}$, **retaining only the Top-$K_{\text{echo}}=32$ keys per query row (`wiener_mmse_topk32`, `exp60` Part 1)** preserves **`99.77%` of $\|W^{\text{MMSE}}\|_F^2$** at Step 120 (`98.71%` at Step 0) and matches dense $T \times T$ `wiener_mmse` key cosine to within `0.0001` (`0.1020` vs. `0.1021`).
   - **5-Matrix Spherical Pre-Polar Muon for `attn.c_q` (`2.0682 -> 2.0641 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB) & Factorized Controls (`exp45`, `Proposition 5`):** Because `q_r = norm(apply_rotary_emb(q, cos, sin))` is $0$-homogeneous in $\|W_Q^{(l)}\|_F$, standard unconstrained `Pre-Polar Muon` updates (`wm_G2048_codesigned_active_q_snr_matched_triton`, $\eta_Q = 0.001$: `2.0681 ± 0.0006`) or properly bound per-head scalar temperatures (`wm_G2048_codesigned_per_head_temp_triton`, where `block.attn.head_log_temp` is bound to the module for evaluation parity: `2.0681 ± 0.0006`) leave DEV BPB virtually unchanged (`-0.0001` BPB). By combining (i) unit-RMS tangent query perturbation, (ii) spherical Frobenius retraction $\|W_Q^{(l)}\|_F \equiv \|W_{Q, 0}^{(l)}\|_F$, (iii) high momentum $\beta_Q = 0.95$, and (iv) $\sqrt{d_h}$-scaled angular step size $\eta_Q = \sqrt{d_h}\,\eta_V = 0.048$, `wm_G2048_codesigned_spherical_active_q_triton` (`25,460,736 / 26,346,060 = 96.64%` active parameters) reaches **`2.0641 / 2.0636 / 2.0647` $\to$ `2.0641 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`2.0273 ± 0.0005` VAL-B BPB, `289.43` PPL)**—improving upon the 4-matrix baseline by **`-0.0041` BPB (`-3.23` PPL)** and capturing **`45.6%`** of the exact-BP 4-to-5-matrix $W_Q$-unfreezing gain (`2.0532 -> 2.0442`, `-0.0090` BPB). To factorize the geometric vs. hyperparameter components cleanly, we evaluate two matched-hyperparameter ($\beta_Q = 0.95, \eta_Q = 0.048$) Euclidean controls in `exp45`: (a) `wm_G2048_codesigned_euclidean_active_q_matched_hp_triton` (`spherical_qk = False, unit_rms_qk_pert = False, tangent_proj_qk = True`), which removes spherical Frobenius retraction and unit-RMS perturbation scaling while retaining tangent projection (`2.0648 / 2.0643 / 2.0655` $\to$ **`2.0649 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB, `290.02` PPL**); and (b) `wm_G2048_codesigned_unprojected_active_q_matched_hp_triton` (`tangent_proj_qk = False, spherical_qk = False, unit_rms_qk_pert = False`), which additionally removes query tangent projection (`2.0648 / 2.0644 / 2.0655` $\to$ **`2.0649 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB, `290.03` PPL**). Both controls attribute `-0.0033` BPB of the `-0.0041` BPB gain to step-size/momentum matching ($\eta_Q=0.048, \beta_Q=0.95$) and `-0.0008` BPB specifically to spherical Frobenius retraction + unit-RMS perturbation scaling; consistent with **Proposition 5**, while unprojected reward reconstruction retains single-site radial noise of relative energy $\approx 1/d_h = 1.53\%$ at $d_h=64$ (`exp49` Part 1), the projected and unprojected `exp45` arms are empirically nearly indistinguishable (`2.0649` vs. `2.0649`).
4. **Full 6-Matrix (`26,345,472 / 26,346,060 = 99.9978%` Active), Exploratory Active-`ve_gate` (`26,346,048 = 100%` Matrix Weights Active), $M \in \{2, 4\}$ Amortized Multi-Probe Probing, Wiener-MMSE / Hadamard-$4$ / Sparse Top-$K=32$ Key Gather (`Proposition 6`, `exp59`, `exp60`), $2 \times 2$ Depth Factorial, and Same-Trainer Matched-Training-Time Controls (`exp47`, `exp49`–`exp51`, `exp59`, `exp60`):**
   In `exp45`, naively activating both $W_Q$ and $W_K$ (`wm_G2048_codesigned_spherical_active_qk_triton`: `2.0669 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB) degraded performance relative to 5-matrix Active-$W_Q$ (`2.0641 ± 0.0006`). Across `exp47` Part 2, `exp49` Part 2, `exp50`, `exp51`, `exp59`, and `exp60` (`45` three-seed arms = `135` runs on ClimbMix `d6`, where 6-matrix arms without active gates train `26,345,472 / 26,346,060 = 99.9978%` of parameters—leaving the `576` `ve_gate` weights of shape $6 \times 32$ at zero-based blocks `1, 3, 5` and `12` residual scalars frozen—and active-`ve_gate` arms train `26,346,048 / 26,346,060 = 99.99995%` = `100%` of matrix weights), we diagnose and resolve all three causes of that degradation and compare fixed-token vs. matched-training-region-time performance:
   - **Cause 1 — Cross-Probe Perturbation Contamination in Joint SDPA (`-0.0014` BPB Main Effect from Decoupling):** Following the separate attention-internal query and key jittering principle of Dust [9] (`Section 2.3`), perturbing both queries and keys simultaneously in a single FlashAttention call `sdpa(q_r_p, k_r_p, v)` produces $\Delta y_{qk} = \Delta y_q + \Delta y_k + O(\sigma^2)$, so the query reward $R_q \propto \langle w_{\text{head}}, \Delta y_{qk}\rangle$ suffers cross-probe interference from the independent key perturbation $\Delta y_k(\xi_k)$ (and vice versa). Evaluating **decoupled local attention probes** $\Delta y_q = \text{sdpa}(q_{\text{pert}}, k_{\text{clean}}, v_{\text{clean}}) - y_{\text{clean}}$ and $\Delta y_k = \text{sdpa}(q_{\text{clean}}, k_{\text{pert}}, v_{\text{clean}}) - y_{\text{clean}}$ during the originating forward pass ($t$, performing $1$ clean attention evaluation plus $2M$ perturbed attention evaluations per active block) and consuming them two steps later ($t-2$) eliminates cross-probe contamination: at $\gamma_K = 0.0, \eta_K = 0.012$, moving from coupled same-position (`2.0657 / 2.0657 / 2.0665` $\to$ `2.0660 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB) to decoupled same-position (`2.0649 / 2.0641 / 2.0650` $\to$ `2.0647 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB) improves 6-matrix learning by **`-0.0013` BPB**, and at $\gamma_K = 0.85, \eta_K = 0.012$, moving from coupled future-accumulated (`2.0646 / 2.0646 / 2.0647` $\to$ `2.0646 ± 0.0001` DEV BPB) to decoupled future-accumulated (`2.0640 / 2.0629 / 2.0625` $\to$ **`2.0631 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB, `2.0264 ± 0.0011` VAL-B BPB, `288.64` PPL**) improves learning by **`-0.0015` BPB** (or `-0.0018` BPB over step-multiplexed alternating $W_Q/W_K$ steps `2.0649 ± 0.0007`).
   - **Cause 2 — Key Truncation vs. Discounted Future-Query Accumulation & SNR-Matched Key Step Size (`-0.0015` BPB Main Effect):** Combining SNR-matched key learning rate $\eta_K = 0.012, \beta_K = 0.98$ with discounted future-query key accumulation improves decoupled 6-matrix learning from `2.0647 ± 0.0005` ($\gamma_K = 0.00$) to **`2.0638 / 2.0635 / 2.0624` $\to$ `2.0632 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`288.73` PPL)** at $\gamma_K = 0.98$ and **`2.0640 / 2.0629 / 2.0625` $\to$ `2.0631 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`288.64` PPL)** at $\gamma_K = 0.85$, surpassing 5-matrix Active-$W_Q$ (`2.0641 ± 0.0006`) by **`-0.0010` BPB** at $M=1$.
   - **Amortized Multi-Probe ($M \in \{2, 4\}$) Local $(W_Q, W_K)$ Probing, Pre-Rotary Reward-Side Tangent Projection (`Proposition 5`), Closed-Form Wiener-MMSE / Hadamard-$4$ / Sparse Top-$K=32$ Key Gather (`Proposition 6`, `exp59`, `exp60`), & Exploratory Active `ve_gate` (`2.0631 -> 2.0552 ± 0.0007 -> 2.0519 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB, `exp47` + `exp49` + `exp59` + `exp60`):** Because the clean pre-synaptic input $x_{\text{attn\_in}}(t-2)$ and outgoing receiver columns $W_{\text{attn\_proj}}^{(l)}(t)$ are shared across local attention probes on the same clean forward trunk, averaging $M$ independent Rademacher query/key probes inside the post-synaptic modulation vector $\bar{u}^{(1)} = \frac{1}{M}\sum_{r=1}^M R^{(1, r)} (\xi^{(1, r)}/\sigma)$ *before* the single **Stage-1** outer-product `bmm` (`-(bar_u.t() @ x_attn_in) / BT`) and single `Pre-Polar Muon` orthogonalization step reduces conditional probe sampling variance by $1/M$ (given shared activations, readers, and downstream credit) with **zero additional Stage-1 outer-product matmuls or Muon steps**. Across Seeds `46, 47, 48`, $M=2$ decoupled 6-matrix Delayed WM reaches **`2.0589 / 2.0596 / 2.0604` $\to$ `2.0596 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`2.0228 ± 0.0006` VAL-B BPB, `285.87` PPL)** (`wm_d6_6mat_decoupled_qk_future_g085_m2_lrk012_triton`), and **`2.0602 / 2.0595 / 2.0589` $\to$ `2.0595 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`2.0227 ± 0.0008` VAL-B BPB, `285.81` PPL)** (`wm_d6_6mat_decoupled_qk_future_g085_m2_rng_matched_lrk012_triton`, which draws probes $r \ge 2$ from isolated CUDA RNG generators so the main model/data RNG stream is bit-for-bit identical to $M=1$). Note that in the code, Rademacher signs $\xi_q, \xi_k \in \{-1, +1\}^{d_h}$ are drawn in the **pre-rotary coordinate frame** (`q_pert = q + sigma * rms_q * xi_q`, followed by `q_r_p = norm(apply_rotary_emb(q_pert, cos, sin))`) and projected onto the pre-rotary tangent space $P_{q_n}, P_{k_n}$ at consumption time; because orthogonal RoPE $R_\theta$ commutes with `RMSNorm` and $P_u$ and preserves inner products and covariance trace, Proposition 5's fourth-moment identity applies in that pre-rotary coordinate frame. Removing reward-side tangent projection $P_{q_n}, P_{k_n}$ (`wm_d6_6mat_decoupled_qk_future_g085_m2_no_reward_proj_triton`, `exp49`) scores **`2.0603 / 2.0598 / 2.0593` $\to$ `2.0598 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB (`2.0229 ± 0.0007` VAL-B, `286.03` PPL)** in `10.72s`. Unfreezing the 3 value-embedding gate matrices `ve_gate` (`576` parameters of shape $6 \times 32$ across zero-based blocks `1, 3, 5`, `wm_d6_6mat_plus_ve_gate_m2_rng_matched_triton`, `exp49`, so `26,346,048 / 26,346,060 = 99.99995%` of total parameters and `100%` of matrix weights are active, leaving only `12` residual scalars frozen; gate perturbation is credited to Dust [9], Section 2.3) yields **`2.0590 / 2.0593 / 2.0589` $\to$ `2.0591 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB (`2.0221 ± 0.0003` VAL-B, `285.43` PPL)** in `11.12s`; because its paired difference relative to the isolated-RNG $M=2$ reference is $-0.00047 \pm 0.00064$ BPB (seedwise differences $-0.0012, -0.0002, 0.0000$), we treat the `ve_gate` improvement as an exploratory completeness check rather than a robust general gain. Scaling amortized local probing to $M=4$ (`wm_d6_6mat_decoupled_qk_future_g085_m4_rng_matched_lrk012_triton`, `exp49`) reaches **`2.0545 / 2.0553 / 2.0558` $\to$ `2.0552 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`2.0183 ± 0.0005` VAL-B BPB, `282.43` PPL)** in `13.94s` (paired improvement $-0.00433 \pm 0.00131$ BPB over $M=2$)—improving upon 4-matrix Delayed WM (`2.0682 ± 0.0006`) by **`-0.0130` BPB (`-10.23` PPL)**, reaching numerical proximity (**`+0.0020` BPB**, rather than statistical equivalence) to the 4-matrix Exact Backpropagation reference (`2.0532 ± 0.0008`), and capturing **`60.2%`** (`0.0130 / 0.0216`, vs. **`40.3%`** = `0.0087 / 0.0216` at $M=2$) of the full 360-step 4-to-6-matrix exact-BP gain (`2.0532 -> 2.0316`). Replacing fixed exponential decay $\gamma_K = 0.85$ at $M=4$ with causal attention-weighted `Rake` ($W_{\tau, s}=A_{\tau, s}$), the closed-form **Wiener-MMSE Key-Gather filter** ($W_{\tau, s}^{\text{MMSE}}(4) = \frac{4 A_{\tau, s}^2}{3 A_{\tau, s}^2 + \|A_{\tau, :}\|_2^2}$, `Proposition 6`, `exp59`), **Walsh-Hadamard $H_4$ Temporal Probe Multiplexing** (`c(s, i) = (i + (i // 32) + s) mod 4`, eliminating `48/63 = 76.2%` of intra-head cross-coordinate interference pairs including all 32 RoPE rotary pairs, `exp60`), and **Sparse Top-$K_{\text{echo}}=32$ `Wiener-MMSE` Key Gather** (`exp60`) further improves 360-step accuracy: under the exact `exp49` update law (`exact_exp49_opt=True`, $\eta_K=0.024$), Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=4$ `wiener_mmse` (`wm_d6_upper_half_wiener_mmse_key_m4_lrk024_exact_exp49_360steps_triton`, `exp59` Arm 9) reaches **`2.0551 / 2.0531 / 2.0537` $\to$ `2.0540 ± 0.0010` DEV BPB (`2.0170 ± 0.0012` VAL-B, `281.48` PPL, `11.18s`, `-0.0022` BPB over Upper-Half $\gamma_K=0.85$ `2.0562`)**, Full 6-Matrix $M=4$ `rake_attn` (`wm_d6_6mat_rake_attn_key_m4_lrk024_exact_exp49_360steps_triton`, `exp59` Arm 7) reaches **`2.0532 / 2.0527 / 2.0527` $\to$ `2.0529 ± 0.0003` DEV BPB (`2.0158 ± 0.0005` VAL-B, `280.63` PPL, `14.52s`)**, Sparse Top-$K=32$ `wiener_mmse_topk32` (`wm_d6_6mat_wiener_mmse_topk32_key_m4_360steps_triton`, `exp60` Arm 2) reaches **`2.0533 / 2.0514 / 2.0519` $\to$ `2.0522 ± 0.0010` DEV BPB (`2.0152 ± 0.0012` VAL-B, `280.12` PPL, `12.16s`)**, dense `wiener_mmse` (`wm_d6_6mat_wiener_mmse_key_m4_lrk024_exact_exp49_360steps_triton`, `exp59` Arm 8) reaches **`2.0531 / 2.0514 / 2.0519` $\to$ `2.0521 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB (`2.0151 ± 0.0010` VAL-B, `280.04` PPL, `14.71s`)**, and `wiener_mmse` + Hadamard-$4$ (`wm_d6_6mat_wiener_mmse_hadamard4_qk_m4_360steps_triton` and `wm_d6_6mat_wiener_mmse_hadamard4_lev_inv_m4_360steps_triton`, `exp60` Arms 1 & 3) reaches **`2.0524 / 2.0508 / 2.0524` $\to$ `2.0519 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB (`2.0147 ± 0.0009` VAL-B, `279.83` PPL, `12.20s`)**—improving over $\gamma_K=0.85$ (`2.0552 ± 0.0007`) by **`-0.0033` BPB** across all 3 seeds, recovering **`66.2%` (`-0.0163 / -0.0216` BPB)** of the 360-step 4-to-6-matrix Exact-BP gain (`2.0532 -> 2.0316`), and reaching `-0.0013` DEV BPB below the 4-matrix Exact-BP reference (`2.0532 ± 0.0008`, while still trailing 6-matrix Exact BP `2.0316 ± 0.0009` by `+0.0203` BPB; under the alternative weight-row-projected non-Nesterov EMA loop [`exact_exp49_opt=False`, `exp59` Arms 1–2], 360-step `rake_attn` scores `2.0548 ± 0.0007` at $\eta_K=0.012$ and `2.0545 ± 0.0008` at $\eta_K=0.024$).
   - **720-Step Fixed-Token Scaling ($M=2$, Pure $M=4$ vs. $M=4+\text{ve\_gate}$, and `Rake` vs. `Wiener-MMSE` / `Hadamard-4` / `Sparse Top-K=32` Key Gather), Main-RNG-Matched & Layer-RNG-Synchronized $2 \times 2$ Block-Depth Factorials, Fixed-Probe Upper-Half $M=4$ Reallocation, Compositional/Bus/Clock/Leverage-Inversion Controls, and Same-Trainer Matched-Training-Region-Time Controls (`exp49`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp54`, `exp56`–`exp60`):**
     1. **720-Step Fixed-Token Scaling, Pure $M=4$ vs. `ve_gate` Isolation, Closed-Form `Wiener-MMSE` / `Hadamard-4` / `Sparse Top-K=32` Key Gather (`1.9632 ± 0.0006` dense in `29.14s`; `1.9633 ± 0.0009` sparse `Top-K=32` in `24.36s`; `1.9632 ± 0.0009` `Hadamard-4 + LevInv` in `24.54s`), & Exact-BP Recovery (`exp49`, `exp51`, `exp53`, `exp59`, `exp60`):** At `720` fixed steps (`1.475M` tokens), evaluating 4-matrix vs. 6-matrix Exact Backpropagation under the co-designed optimizer (`backprop_d6_4mat_720steps_codesigned` vs. `backprop_d6_6mat_720steps_codesigned`, `exp51`) shows that the exact-BP 4-to-6-matrix $(W_Q, W_K)$-unfreezing gain widens **$2.40\times$ from `-0.0216` BPB (`2.0532 -> 2.0316`) at 360 steps to `-0.0519` BPB (`1.9423 ± 0.0031 -> 1.8904 ± 0.0012` DEV BPB; `1.9072 ± 0.0036 -> 1.8551 ± 0.0016` VAL-B) at 720 steps**. Under backward-free Delayed WM, 720-step 4-matrix WM (`wm_d6_4mat_same_trainer_720steps_triton`, `exp51`) scores **`1.9990 / 1.9982 / 1.9996` $\to$ `1.9989 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`1.9646 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `241.96` PPL)** in `15.62s` (matching `exp39`'s `1.9989 ± 0.0008` in `13.57s`), 720-step Full 6-Matrix $M=2$ reaches **`1.9772 / 1.9762 / 1.9755` $\to$ `1.9763 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB (`1.9416 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `227.41` PPL)** in `21.67s` (`exp49`) and **`1.9750 / 1.9742 / 1.9775` $\to$ `1.9756 ± 0.0017` DEV BPB (`1.9408 ± 0.0019` VAL-B, `226.95` PPL)** in `21.77s` inside the main-RNG-matched `exp50` trainer (`wm_d6_full_6block_qk_m2_isolated_rng_720steps_triton`, `exp53`, widening the fixed-token gain over 4-matrix WM to **`-0.0226` to `-0.0233` BPB = `43.5%`–`44.9%` of the 720-step BP gain**). At $M=4$ under $\gamma_K=0.85$, while 720-step Full 6-Matrix $M=4 + \text{ve\_gate}$ (`wm_d6_6mat_decoupled_qk_future_g085_m4_plus_ve_gate_720steps_triton`, `exp51`) reaches **`1.9675 / 1.9686 / 1.9687` $\to$ `1.9683 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`1.9334 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `222.45` PPL)** in `28.57s` (`59.0%` of the BP gain), isolating **pure 6-matrix $M=4$ without `ve_gate`** (`wm_d6_6mat_decoupled_qk_future_g085_m4_no_gate_720steps_triton`, `exp53` Arm 2) reaches **`1.9671 / 1.9668 / 1.9677` $\to$ `1.9672 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB (`1.9324 ± 0.0005` VAL-B, `221.80` PPL)** in **`27.71s`** (**`-0.0317` BPB over 4-matrix WM = `61.1%` of the 720-step BP gain**, outperforming $M=4+\text{ve\_gate}$ by `-0.0011 ± 0.0007` BPB across all 3 seeds and confirming that $M: 2 \to 4$ drives the entire 720-step improvement). Furthermore, at 720 steps where attention heads sharpen, `exp59` and `exp60` directly confirm Proposition 6's predictions: under the exact `exp49` update law (`exact_exp49_opt=True`, $\eta_K=0.024$), 720-step Full 6-Matrix $M=4$ `rake_attn` (`wm_d6_6mat_rake_attn_key_m4_lrk024_exact_exp49_720steps_triton`, `exp59` Arm 11) reaches **`1.9668 / 1.9648 / 1.9659` $\to$ `1.9658 ± 0.0010` DEV BPB (`1.9310 ± 0.0010` VAL-B, `220.96` PPL, `28.97s`)**, dense `wiener_mmse` (`wm_d6_6mat_wiener_mmse_key_m4_lrk024_exact_exp49_720steps_triton`, `exp59` Arm 12) reaches **`1.9635 / 1.9626 / 1.9636` $\to$ `1.9632 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`1.9284 ± 0.0006` VAL-B, `219.39` PPL, `29.14s`)**, `wiener_mmse` + Hadamard-$4$ (`wm_d6_6mat_wiener_mmse_hadamard4_qk_m4_720steps_triton`, `exp60` Arm 4) reaches **`1.9642 / 1.9627 / 1.9632` $\to$ `1.9634 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`1.9284 ± 0.0007` VAL-B, `219.46` PPL, `24.52s`)**, `wiener_mmse` + Hadamard-$4$ + Stage-2 Leverage Inversion (`wm_d6_6mat_wiener_mmse_hadamard4_lev_inv_m4_720steps_triton`, `exp60` Arm 6) reaches **`1.9641 / 1.9623 / 1.9632` $\to$ `1.9632 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB (`1.9283 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `219.37` PPL, `24.54s`)**, and **Sparse Top-$K=32$ `wiener_mmse_topk32`** (`wm_d6_6mat_wiener_mmse_topk32_key_m4_720steps_triton`, `exp60` Arm 5) reaches **`1.9640 / 1.9622 / 1.9636` $\to$ `1.9633 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB (`1.9285 ± 0.0009` VAL-B, `219.42` PPL)** in **`24.36s` (`16.4%` faster than dense `wiener_mmse` `29.14s` while matching its `1.9632` DEV BPB to within `0.0001` BPB!)**—improving over `rake_attn` by **`-0.0025` to `-0.0026` BPB** across all 3 seeds, improving over $\gamma_K=0.85$ (`1.9672 ± 0.0005`) by **`-0.0039` to `-0.0040` DEV BPB (`-2.41` to `-2.43` PPL)** across all 3 seeds, and lifting 720-step 4-to-6-matrix Exact-BP attention-routing recovery from **`61.1%` (`-0.0317 / -0.0519`) to `68.8%` (`-0.0357 / -0.0519` BPB)** (while under the alternative weight-row-projected non-Nesterov EMA loop [`exact_exp49_opt=False`, `exp59` Arms 3–4], 720-step `rake_attn` scores `1.9733 ± 0.0014` on Upper-Half $M=4$ and `1.9697 ± 0.0011` on Full 6-Matrix $M=4$).
     2. **Why Full-Depth SDPA Probing Loses to 4-Matrix at Matched Training-Region Time (`exp49`–`exp51`):** Inside the `exp49`–`exp51` trainer, evaluating $2M$ perturbed SDPA calls across all 6 blocks increases per-step training-region time from `21.46–21.65 ms/step` (4-matrix) to `22.83 ms/step` ($M=1$) and `25.97–30.10 ms/step` ($M=2$). At the short 360-step budget, 4-matrix controls given the same measured training-region GPU time (`384` steps in `8.20s` vs. `8.22s` for $M=1$; `438` steps in `9.39s` vs. `9.35s` for $M=2$) reach **`2.0598 ± 0.0005`** and **`2.0454 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB**, outperforming 360-step 6-matrix $M=1$ (`2.0631`) and $M=2$ (`2.0595`). At the longer 720-step horizon, while 720-step Full 6-Matrix $M=2$ (`1.9763 ± 0.0009` in `21.67s` / `1.9756 ± 0.0017` in `21.77s`) beats an `875`-step 4-matrix control (`wm_d6_4mat_equal_compute_720m2_875steps_triton`, `exp50`, calibrated to the 360-step $+21.5\%$ step-time ratio: `1.9819 / 1.9815 / 1.9828` $\to$ **`1.9821 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB, `1.9485 ± 0.0006` VAL-B, `231.03` PPL** in `18.91s`) by `-0.0058` to `-0.0065` DEV BPB, granting 4-matrix the **full `21.55s` (`1,004` steps)** inside the same trainer (`wm_d6_4mat_equal_time_720m2_1004steps_triton`, `exp51`) reaches **`1.9718 / 1.9715 / 1.9726` $\to$ `1.9720 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`1.9392 ± 0.0004` VAL-B, `224.73` PPL)** in `21.55s`—still edging out 720-step Full 6-Matrix $M=2$ (`1.9763` / `1.9756`) by `-0.0036` to `-0.0043` BPB.
      3. **Main-RNG-Matched & Per-Layer-RNG-Synchronized 720-Step $2 \times 2$ `{Lower-Half (0,1,2), Upper-Half (3,4,5)}` Depth Factorials, Fixed-Probe Upper-Half $M=4$ Reallocation (`1.9699 ± 0.0001` [`g085`] & `1.9672 ± 0.0017` [`wiener_mmse`]), and Same-Trainer Matched-Time Advantage (`1.9670 ± 0.0002` at 740st $M=4$; `1.9609 ± 0.0006` at 840st $M=2$, `exp50`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp54`, `exp56`, `exp59`):** Why does uniform 6-block probing underperform at matched training-region time? Evaluating our 720-step $2 \times 2$ depth-ablation factorial both in `train_d6_exp50_arm` (`exp51` + `exp53` Arm 1, $M=2$, where query/key draws use isolated CUDA RNGs so the **main model/data RNG stream is bit-for-bit identical** across all 4 arms) and in `train_d6_layer_rng_synced_arm` (`exp56` Arms 11–12, which additionally advances the query/key generators at skipped blocks so **every active block's $(\xi_q, \xi_k)$ perturbation tensors are also bit-for-bit identical** to Full 6-Block $M=2$) reveals a consistent depth gradient in attention-routing utility:
         - **4-Matrix (`none` active Q/K, `24.58M` active):** `1.9990 / 1.9982 / 1.9996` $\to$ `1.9989 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`1.9646 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `241.96` PPL, `15.62s` run GPU)
         - **Lower-Half Blocks `(0, 1, 2)` Only (`25.46M` active):** `1.9826 / 1.9829 / 1.9841` $\to$ **`1.9832 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`1.9487 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `231.75` PPL)** in `18.90s` (`exp51` main-RNG-matched, `-0.0157` BPB over 4-matrix); and `1.9849 / 1.9826 / 1.9852` $\to$ **`1.9842 ± 0.0014` DEV BPB (`1.9497 ± 0.0014` VAL-B, `232.42` PPL)** in `16.06s` (`exp56` layer-RNG-synced, `-0.0147` BPB over 4-matrix)
         - **Long-Window `L` Blocks `(3, 5)` Only (`wm_d6_long_window_35_qk_m2_720steps_triton`, `25.17M` active):** `1.9802 / 1.9793 / 1.9788` $\to$ **`1.9794 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`1.9449 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `229.36` PPL)** in `17.82s` (`-0.0195` BPB over 4-matrix with only 2 blocks probed!)
         - **Upper-Half Blocks `(3, 4, 5)` Only (`25.46M` active):** `1.9786 / 1.9775 / 1.9775` $\to$ **`1.9779 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`1.9431 ± 0.0007` VAL-B, `228.40` PPL)** in **`18.59s`** (`exp51` main-RNG-matched, **`-0.0210` BPB over 4-matrix = `90.1%`–`92.9%` of the full 6-block gain at half the SDPA probe overhead**); and `1.9757 / 1.9757 / 1.9793` $\to$ **`1.9769 ± 0.0021` DEV BPB (`1.9421 ± 0.0023` VAL-B, `227.78` PPL)** in `16.09s` (`exp56` layer-RNG-synced, **`-0.0220` BPB = `94.4%` of the full 6-block gain**!)
         - **Full 6-Block `(0..5)` (`wm_d6_full_6block_qk_m2_isolated_rng_720steps_triton`, `exp53` Arm 1, & `wm_d6_6mat_decoupled_qk_future_g085_m2_720steps_triton`, `exp49`, `26.35M` active):** `1.9750 / 1.9742 / 1.9775` $\to$ **`1.9756 ± 0.0017` DEV BPB (`1.9408 ± 0.0019` VAL-B, `226.95` PPL)** in `21.77s` (`exp53` main/layer-RNG-matched) and `1.9772 / 1.9762 / 1.9755` $\to$ `1.9763 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB (`1.9416 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `227.41` PPL) in `21.67s` (`exp49`).
         *(Note on interpretation: Upper-half versus lower-half probing also alters the attention-window composition across the SSSLSL layout—where blocks `3, 5` are full-context `L` and block `4` is short-window `S`, whereas blocks `0, 1, 2` are all short-window `S`).*
         Thus, **once Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $(W_Q, W_K)$ is active, also probing Lower-Half `(0, 1, 2)` adds only `-0.0013` to `-0.0023` DEV BPB while costing `+3.08s–3.18s` (`+16.6%` training-region time)**. We test two distinct ways to reallocate that saved compute:
         - **Fixed-Token (`720` Steps), Fixed-24-SDPA-Probe/Step Reallocation (`wm_d6_upper_half_qk_m4_720steps_triton`, `exp53` Arm 3; `wm_d6_upper_half_wiener_mmse_key_m4_lrk024_exact_exp49_720steps_triton`, `exp59` Arm 10; & `740`-Step Extension `exp54` Arm 3):** Concentrating the exact same 24 perturbed SDPA calls/step on Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` ($M=4$ on blocks `3, 4, 5`; $M=0$ on blocks `0, 1, 2`) at **fixed `720` steps** achieves **`1.9700 / 1.9698 / 1.9698` $\to$ `1.9699 ± 0.0001` DEV BPB (`1.9349 ± 0.0001` VAL-B, `223.42` PPL)** in **`21.05s`** under $\gamma_K=0.85$ (`exp53` Arm 3)—outperforming uniform 720-step Full 6-Block $M=2$ (`1.9756 ± 0.0017` in `21.77s` / `1.9763 ± 0.0009` in `21.67s`) by **`-0.0057` to `-0.0064` DEV BPB** at equal tokens and lower time, and outperforming 1,004-step 4-matrix (`1.9720 ± 0.0006` in `21.55s`) by **`-0.0021` DEV BPB (`-0.0043` VAL-B BPB)** in `0.50s` less time—and under `wiener_mmse` (`exp59` Arm 10) achieves **`1.9690 / 1.9656 / 1.9670` $\to$ `1.9672 ± 0.0017` DEV BPB (`1.9322 ± 0.0017` VAL-B, `221.79` PPL)** in **`22.36s`**, matching all-6-block $M=4$ $\gamma_K=0.85$ (`1.9672 ± 0.0005` in `27.71s`) at half the SDPA probes! Running Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=4$ ($\gamma_K=0.85$) for `740` steps (`wm_d6_upper_half_qk_m4_equal_time_740steps_triton`, `exp54` Arm 3, `19.19s`) reaches **`1.9669 / 1.9669 / 1.9673` $\to$ `1.9670 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB (`1.9321 ± 0.0002` VAL-B, `221.69` PPL)**.
         - **Same-Trainer `~21.6s` Matched-Time Token Extension (`wm_d6_upper_half_qk_m2_equal_time_840steps_triton`, `exp51`):** Spending the saved `3.12s` on `120` additional steps of Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=2$ (`840` steps in **`21.71s`**) achieves **`1.9615 / 1.9609 / 1.9604` $\to$ `1.9609 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`1.9264 ± 0.0007` VAL-B BPB, `218.03` PPL)**—**outperforming 1,004-step 4-matrix (`1.9720 ± 0.0006` in `21.55s`) by `-0.0111` DEV BPB (`-0.0128` VAL-B BPB, `-6.70` PPL) and outperforming 720-step Full 6-Matrix $M=2$ (`1.9763 ± 0.0009` in `21.67s`) by `-0.0154` DEV BPB (`-0.0152` VAL-B BPB, `-9.38` PPL) at matched training-region GPU time!**
         - **Stage-2 Terminal-Bus Multi-Probe (`M_bus = 2`), Stage-2 `RMSNorm` Leverage Inversion (`exp60` Arms 3 & 6), 720-Step `both_stages_pre_install` Clock Controls (`exp59` Arms 5–6), Same-Clock Compositional-Relay Diagnostic Controls (`exp53` Arm 4, `exp54` Arms 1, 2, 4), and Idealized Block-Skip vs. Sub-Block Direct-Skip Surrogate BP & Exact-`exp49`-Optimizer Controls (`exp57`, `exp58`):** Adding a second independent Rademacher probe at the terminal `RMSNorm` bus (`wm_d6_upper_half_qk_m4_bus_m2_720steps_triton`, `exp54` Arm 2: `1.9702 / 1.9699 / 1.9699` $\to$ **`1.9700 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB, `1.9350 ± 0.0001` VAL-B** in `19.25s`) matches single-probe `M_bus = 1` (`1.9699 ± 0.0001`) to within `0.0001` BPB, which is compatible with Proposition 1(c)'s result that Stage-2 cross-coordinate `RMSNorm` variance is $O(1/D) \approx 0.0026$ at $D=384$. Likewise, at 720 steps under the co-designed optimizer ($\epsilon = 10^{-4}, \text{lrm}: 1.0 \to 0.2$, `exp59` Arms 5–6), `both_stages_pre_install` (`Stage 2: (t-1, t-1, t), Stage 1: (t-2, t-1, t)`) scores **`1.9979 / 1.9970 / 1.9983` $\to$ `1.9977 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`1.9635 ± 0.0006` VAL-B, `241.18` PPL, `22.63s`)** on 4-matrix (`-0.0012` BPB vs. `strict_post_install` `1.9989 ± 0.0007`) and **`1.9687 / 1.9693 / 1.9697` $\to$ `1.9692 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB (`1.9346 ± 0.0004` VAL-B, `223.02` PPL, `39.77s`)** on 6-matrix $M=4$ (`+0.0020` BPB vs. `strict_post_install` `1.9672 ± 0.0005`), confirming that $\epsilon = 10^{-4}$ plus drift annealing keeps post-installation drift bounded within `0.0020` BPB at 720 steps. Similarly, augmenting Stage-2 (`mlp.c_proj, attn.c_proj`) terminal-skip credit with outgoing-column WM rewards from downstream blocks' `mlp.c_fc` matrices (constructed in a descending block loop at clock $t$ from origin-$t-2$ cached traces and supplied to earlier blocks' Stage-2 writer accumulators within the same construction call prior to `mlp.c_fc` installation, and therefore classified separately as `same-clock compositional-relay diagnostic controls` rather than strictly delayed zero-relay WM arms) yields `1.9779 ± 0.0006` (`exp53` Arm 4, shared `xi_bus`, $\alpha_{\text{comp}}=0.20$, `19.37s`), `1.9780 ± 0.0008` (`exp54` Arm 1, independent $\xi_{\text{comp}} \perp \xi_{\text{bus}}$, $\alpha_{\text{comp}}=0.05$, `20.25s`), and `1.9787 ± 0.0008` (`exp54` Arm 4, RMS-norm-matched $\alpha_{\text{comp}}=0.15 \|\hat{g}_{\text{bus}}\|/\|\hat{g}_{\text{comp}}\|$, `19.49s`) vs. `1.9779 ± 0.0006` for pure terminal-skip routing ($\alpha_{\text{comp}}=0$)—showing that two-hop Rademacher variance is roughly $12\times$ smaller in RMS norm than single-hop terminal-skip credit and slightly degrades Stage-2 writer alignment when scaled up. To compare our 3-stage pipeline against idealized direct-to-head backpropagation surrogates while explicitly accounting for (a) the Proposition 1(c) coordinate-energy factor $\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[P_x\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}}^{(1)}] = \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z)}P_x\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_i^2/D)a \neq \frac{1}{\text{rms}(z)}P_x a = \mu_{\text{skip}}$ (verified by exact 8-pattern enumeration at $D=3, x=(\sqrt{3/2},\sqrt{3/2},0)^\top, a=(1,0,1)^\top$, where $\mathbb{E}[P_x\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}}^{(1)}]=(1/4,-1/4,1)^\top$ vs. $\mu_{\text{skip}}=(1/2,-1/2,1)^\top$, $\cos = 0.962250$ in `exp58`, whereas applying the closed-form Stage-2 leverage inversion $\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_i^2/D)^{-1}$ prior to $P_x$ restores exact equality $\mathbb{E}[P_x\operatorname{diag}(1 - x_i^2/D)^{-1}\hat{\mu}_{\text{WM}}^{(1)}] = \mu_{\text{skip}}$ with $\cos = 1.000000$ in `exp60` Part 1 and yields `2.0519 ± 0.0009` vs. `2.0519 ± 0.0009` at 360st and `1.9632 ± 0.0009` vs. `1.9634 ± 0.0008` at 720st in `exp60` Arms 3 & 6; without inversion, its modulation-vector discrepancy is bounded by $\left(\frac{\max_i x_i^2}{D}\right)\frac{\|a\|_2}{\text{rms}(z)}$ and its outer-product matrix Frobenius discrepancy by $\left(\frac{\max_i x_i^2}{D}\right)\frac{\|a\|_2 \|h_n\|_2}{\text{rms}(z)}$, so the normalized discrepancy coefficient $\max_i x_i^2/D$ is $O(1/D)$ only under a coordinate-concentration assumption $\max_i x_i^2 = O(1)$, and need not converge with width if coordinates concentrate or if the scale factors $\|a\|_2, \|h_n\|_2$ are not controlled), (b) omitted inter-block vs. intra-block `attn -> mlp` paths (cf. [5]'s fixed-final-discrepancy `block_fixed_top_discrepancy_bp` and intra-block `MLP-Pullback` controls), and (c) hidden-matrix optimizer-law matching, `exp57` and `exp58` evaluate two distinct surrogate losses across three optimizer configurations:
           1. **Sub-Block Direct-Skip Surrogate BP (`forward_terminal_skip_surrogate_loss`, `exp57`) vs. Block-Skip (Intra-Block `attn -> mlp` Preserved) Surrogate BP (`forward_block_skip_keep_intrablock_loss`, `exp58`):** In `forward_terminal_skip_surrogate_loss` (`exp57`), both block entry `curr` and post-attention `curr_after_attn = curr.detach() + attn_out.detach()` are detached before `norm`, removing **both** inter-block composition ($l' > l$) and intra-block attention-to-MLP credit (`attn -> mlp` within block $l$). In `forward_block_skip_keep_intrablock_loss` (`exp58`), `curr_in = curr.detach()` is detached only at block entry while `curr_after_attn = curr_in + attn_out` remains live into `block.mlp(norm(curr_after_attn))` (matching [5]'s fixed-final-discrepancy block-local formulation). At Seed 46 initialization ($D=384, G=2,048, M_g=1$), single-draw WM exhibits high empirical cosine with `g_subblock_skip` (`0.9982` on `wte`, `0.9996` on `attn.c_proj, mlp.c_proj`, `0.9992` on `mlp.c_fc`, `0.9997` on `attn.c_v`; split-half `0.9965–0.9994`), while $\cos(g_{\text{subblock\_skip}}, g_{\text{global}})$ and $\cos(g_{\text{block\_skip}}, g_{\text{global}})$ rise with block depth $l \in \{0, \dots, 5\}$ (from `0.9054 / 0.9050` in `0..2` to `0.9702 / 0.9697` in `3..5` for `g_subblock_skip` on $W_Q, W_K$, and reaching `1.0000` across all matrices at top block $l=5$ under `g_block_skip`; note that this single-seed initialization profile is compatible with greater skip fidelity near the head but does not by itself establish why the trained upper-half recipe retains its reported fraction of the gain). Across 3 seeds (`46, 47, 48`) under the shared-`lr=0.006` co-designed BP optimizer: in the **Writer-Only frozen-internals testbed** (`q, k, v, c_fc, value_embeds, ve_gate` frozen; `10.72M` active, `360` steps), Delayed WM (`2.0702 ± 0.0005` in `4.47s`), Sub-Block-Skip BP (`2.0690 ± 0.0005` in `2.94s`), and Global BP (`2.0633 ± 0.0003` in `5.62s`) lie within `0.0069` BPB. On **4-Matrix**, where `codesigned` (`wm_recipe_matched=False`) **already executes the exact 4-matrix `exp49` update law** (`run_exp49_d6_equal_compute_and_ve_gate.py`, lines 547–566: $b_t = \beta b_{t-1} + (1-\beta)g_t$, Nesterov $v_t = (1-\beta)g_t + \beta b_t$, `PolarExpress(v_t)`, weight decay `0.01`, and identical `lm_head`/`wte`/`ve` optimizers), Global BP $\to$ Block-Skip BP (`exp58`) $\to$ Sub-Block-Skip BP (`exp57`) $\to$ Delayed WM progresses as **`2.0532 ± 0.0008` $\to$ `2.0597 ± 0.0006` $\to$ `2.0600 ± 0.0006` $\to$ `2.0682 ± 0.0006`** at 360 steps, and **`1.9423 ± 0.0031` $\to$ `1.9813 ± 0.0005` $\to$ `1.9819 ± 0.0005` $\to$ `1.9989 ± 0.0007`** at 720 steps (where omitting inter-block paths costs `+0.0390` BPB, omitting intra-block `attn -> mlp` credit costs `+0.0006` BPB, and the exact-optimizer-matched difference between Sub-Block-Skip BP `1.9819` and 4-Matrix Delayed WM `1.9989` is **`+0.0170` BPB**). On **6-Matrix** under shared `lr=0.006`, Global BP $\to$ Block-Skip BP (`exp58`) $\to$ Sub-Block-Skip BP (`exp57`) progresses as **`2.0316 ± 0.0009` $\to$ `2.0458 ± 0.0003` $\to$ `2.0471 ± 0.0003`** at 360 steps, and **`1.8904 ± 0.0012` $\to$ `1.9334 ± 0.0009` $\to$ `1.9367 ± 0.0009`** at 720 steps (where intra-block `attn -> mlp` credit accounts for `+0.0013` and `+0.0033` BPB).
           2. **Exact `exp49` 6-Matrix Update-Law Controls (`exact_exp49_opt=True`, `exp58` Arms 9–12, `exp59` Arms 7–12) vs. Alternative Weight-Row/Sphere-Projected EMA Baselines (`wm_recipe_matched=True`, `exp58` Arms 5–8, `exp59` Arms 1–4):** In `run_exp49_d6_equal_compute_and_ve_gate.py` (`lines 269–270, 547–560`) and `run_exp50_d6_equal_compute_qk_schedules.py`, the 6-matrix Delayed-WM optimizer updates each momentum buffer via $b_t = \beta b_{t-1} + (1-\beta)g_t$, passes the Nesterov mixture $v_t = (1-\beta)g_t + \beta b_t$ to `prepolar_muon_orthogonalize_batched(v_t, ns_steps=5)`, applies decoupled weight decay `p.mul_(1.0 - mlr * lrm * 0.01)` and step `p.add_(u_t, alpha=-(mlr * lrm))`, and for query/key matrices ($\eta_Q=0.048, \beta_Q=0.95, \eta_K \in \{0.012, 0.024\}, \beta_K=0.98$, `is_sph=True`) restores the Frobenius norm `p.mul_(init_norms[id(p)] / p.norm().clamp(min=1e-8))`. Running this **exact `exp49` 6-matrix update law** (`exact_exp49_opt=True`, `exp58` Arms 9–12, verified by source-correspondence and deterministic twin execution in `verify_paper5.py`) improves 360-step 6-Matrix Sub-Block-Skip BP (`subblock_skip_exact_exp49_opt_bp_d6_6mat_360steps`, Arm 9) from `2.0471` (shared `lr=0.006`) to **`2.0157 / 2.0159 / 2.0162` $\to$ `2.0159 ± 0.0003` DEV BPB (`1.9792 ± 0.0002` VAL-B, `253.57` PPL, `9.72s`)**, and at 720 steps yields the unconfounded exact-`exp49`-optimizer 6-Matrix progression: **Global BP (`global_bp_exact_exp49_opt_d6_6mat_720steps`, Arm 12: `1.8620 / 1.8626 / 1.8629` $\to$ `1.8625 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB, `1.8275 ± 0.0007` VAL-B, `166.37` PPL, `20.55s`) $\xrightarrow{+0.0344\text{ (inter-block)}}$ Block-Skip BP (`block_skip_keep_intrablock_exact_exp49_opt_bp_d6_6mat_720steps`, Arm 11: `1.8970 / 1.8961 / 1.8976` $\to$ `1.8969 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB, `1.8619 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `182.86` PPL, `18.85s`) $\xrightarrow{+0.0047\text{ (intra-block)}}$ Sub-Block-Skip BP (`subblock_skip_exact_exp49_opt_bp_d6_6mat_720steps`, Arm 10: `1.9014 / 1.9017 / 1.9018` $\to$ `1.9016 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB, `1.8666 ± 0.0002` VAL-B, `185.26` PPL, `19.47s`) $\xrightarrow{+0.0656\text{ (}\gamma_K=0.85\text{) / }+0.0616\text{ (Wiener-MMSE)}}$ 6-Matrix Delayed WM $M=4$ (`1.9672 ± 0.0005` under $\gamma_K=0.85$ [`exp53`] and `1.9632 ± 0.0006` under `wiener_mmse` [`exp59`])**. Separately, we also report `exp58` Arms 5–8 (`wm_recipe_matched=True`: `2.0205 ± 0.0002` on 360st 6-Mat Sub-Block-Skip; `1.9854 ± 0.0006` on 720st 4-Mat Sub-Block-Skip; `1.9128 ± 0.0009` on 720st 6-Mat Sub-Block-Skip; and `1.8731 ± 0.0007` on 720st 6-Mat Global BP) and `exp59` Arms 1–4 (`2.0548 ± 0.0007` and `2.0545 ± 0.0008` at 360st; `1.9733 ± 0.0014` and `1.9697 ± 0.0011` at 720st), which project core gradients onto weight rows, pass the non-Nesterov EMA buffer alone to `PolarExpress`, and project orthogonalized $Q/K$ updates onto the weight sphere's tangent plane before norm restoration; because these weight-space operations differ from `exp49`'s Nesterov update law, they are classified strictly as **alternative-optimizer baselines** (`1.8731 -> 1.9128 -> 1.9697` internal contrast) rather than `exp49`-optimizer-matched controls.

**Table 5:** 3-seed optimizer co-design, factorized component ablations, 5-matrix Active-$W_Q/W_K$ routing (`exp45_d6_attn_routing_results.json`), full 6-matrix (`26,345,472 / 26,346,060 = 99.9978%` active parameters) joint $(W_Q, W_K)$ routing + $M \in \{2, 4\}$ amortized multi-probe ablations (`exp47`, `exp49`, `exp53`, `exp56`, `exp59`, `exp60`), closed-form parameter-free `Wiener-MMSE`, Sparse `Top-K=32`, Walsh-Hadamard $H_4$ Temporal Multiplexing, Stage-2 `RMSNorm` leverage inversion, and causal attention-weighted `Rake` key gather (`Proposition 6`, `exp59`, `exp60`), 720-step `both_stages_pre_install` clock controls (`exp59`), exploratory active-`ve_gate` vs. pure $M=4$ controls (`26,346,048 = 100%` of matrix weights active), main-RNG-matched and layer-RNG-synchronized 720-step $2 \times 2$ `{Lower-Half, Upper-Half}` depth factorials (`exp51`, `exp53`, `exp56`), fixed-probe Upper-Half $M=4$ reallocation (`exp53`, `exp56`, `exp59`), Stage-2 compositional/bus controls (`exp53`, `exp54`), Writer-Only, Block-Skip (`attn -> mlp` preserved), Sub-Block Direct-Skip, Exact-`exp49`-Optimizer (`exact_exp49_opt`), and Alternative Weight-Row/Sphere-Projected EMA Surrogate/Global BP controls (`exp57`, `exp58`), and same-trainer matched-training-region-time controls (`384, 438, 720, 740, 840, 875, 1,004` steps in `exp49`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp54`) on ClimbMix `d6`, reporting measured 3-seed training-region GPU time (`Run GPU`, forward + update on an uncontended RTX 4090) and 30-step steady-state benchmark estimates (`Est.`).

### 4.7 Comparison with Surrogate-Routed (`SR-LNP`) and Suffix-Recomputed (`SR-NP`) Node Perturbation Across Tokens, Training-Region Time, and Width $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$ (`exp37`, `exp39`, `exp42`, `exp49`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp54`, `exp56`, `exp59`, `exp60`)

Table 6 reports our 3-seed comparison between **Fused Triton Delayed WM** (across 4-matrix, Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=4$, and Full 6-Matrix $M=4$ under both $\gamma_K = 0.85$ and closed-form `wiener_mmse` / `hadamard4`), **Surrogate-Routed Local Node Perturbation (`SR-LNP`, $K \in \{4, 16, 64\}$)**, and **Suffix-Recomputed Node Perturbation (`SR-NP`, $K_{\text{site}}=4, K_{\text{tot}}=76$ suffix-recomputed block draws $+ 24$ local attention draws/step, $\gamma = 0.98$, `exp42`)** across equal token budgets ($360$ steps $= 0.737\text{M}$ tokens), matched training-region GPU time ($500$ steps $= 9.41\text{s}$ run GPU matching `SR-LNP K=16`'s $9.43\text{s}$ in `exp39`, and $720$–$1,004$ steps $= 13.57\text{s}$–$29.14\text{s}$ run GPU vs. `SR-LNP K=64`'s $23.66\text{s}$ across `exp39`, `exp49`–`exp51`, `exp53`–`exp54`, `exp59`, and `exp60`), and model widths $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$ (`exp37_39_master_results.json`, `exp42_faithful_dust_results.json`, `exp49_d6_equal_compute_and_ve_gate_results.json`, `exp51_d6_720step_scaling_and_bp_controls_results.json`, `exp53_d6_compositional_and_rng_controls_results.json`, `exp54_d6_indep_comp_and_bus_multiprobe_results.json`, `exp56_width_scaling_qk_routing_results.json`, `exp59_rake_key_gather_and_clock_controls_results.json`, `exp60_hadamard_qk_multiplexing_and_leverage_inversion_results.json`). We make no claim of superiority over published Dust [9]:
1. **Equal-Token Comparison on `d6` ($360$ Steps, $D=384$):** `wm_G2048_codesigned_opt_triton` (`2.0682 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB in `6.74s` run GPU / `6.72s` est.) outperforms `SR-LNP (K=4)` (`2.3071 ± 0.0016` in `6.32s` / `5.48s` est.) by **`-0.2389` BPB (`-48.1%` PPL)**, `SR-LNP (K=16)` (`2.2603 ± 0.0018` in `9.43s` / `9.23s` est.) by **`-0.1921` BPB (`-41.0%` PPL) in `1.40x` less run GPU time**, `SR-LNP (K=64)` (`2.2041 ± 0.0013` in `23.66s` / `23.22s` est.) by **`-0.1359` BPB (`-31.2%` PPL) in `3.51x` less run GPU time**, and Suffix-Recomputed `SR-NP (K_tot=76)` under `Adam` (`2.4264 ± 0.0088` in `84.38s`) and `Pre-Polar Muon` (`2.2415 ± 0.0015` in `65.19s`) by **`-0.3582` BPB** and **`-0.1733` BPB in `9.67x` less GPU time**. Even at $G=64$ (`5.79s` run GPU / `5.70s` est.), `wm_G64_codesigned_opt_triton` (`2.2415 ± 0.0011`) matches `SR-NP (K_tot=76, Muon)` in $11.3\times$ less GPU time.
2. **Matched Training-Region Time & 720–1,004 Step Horizon Comparison (`9.41s` to `29.14s` Run GPU on `d6`, $D=384$):** In the specialized 4-matrix `exp39` trainer (`~18.8 ms/step`), at matched training-region time to `SR-LNP (K=16)` (`9.41s` vs. `9.43s` run GPU; `9.34s` vs. `9.23s` est., `500` steps), `wm_G2048_codesigned_500steps_match_dustK16_time` reaches **`2.0322 ± 0.0007` DEV BPB (`1.9975 ± 0.0006` VAL-B BPB, `265.13` PPL)**—outperforming `SR-LNP (K=16)` by **`-0.2281` BPB (`-46.6%` PPL)**—and at `720` steps (`13.57s` run GPU / `13.45s` est., `1.74x` faster than `SR-LNP K=64` and `4.80x` faster than `SR-NP K_tot=76`), `wm_G2048_codesigned_720steps_half_dustK64_time` breaks the `2.00` DEV BPB barrier backward-free at **`1.9989 ± 0.0008` DEV BPB (`1.9646 ± 0.0008` VAL-B BPB, `241.96` PPL)**. In the general 6-matrix `exp49`–`exp60` trainer (Table 5), 720-step Full 6-Matrix $M=2$ (`21.67s` / `21.77s`) reaches **`1.9763 ± 0.0009` / `1.9756 ± 0.0017` DEV BPB**, 720-step Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=4$ reaches **`1.9699 ± 0.0001` DEV BPB (`1.9349 ± 0.0001` VAL-B, `223.42` PPL, `21.05s`, $\gamma_K=0.85$)** and **`1.9672 ± 0.0017` DEV BPB (`1.9322 ± 0.0017` VAL-B, `221.79` PPL, `22.36s`, `wiener_mmse`)**, 740-step Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=4$ (`19.19s`) reaches **`1.9670 ± 0.0002` DEV BPB (`1.9321 ± 0.0002` VAL-B, `221.69` PPL)**, 720-step Pure Full 6-Matrix $M=4$ reaches **`1.9672 ± 0.0005` DEV BPB (`1.9324 ± 0.0005` VAL-B, `221.80` PPL, `27.71s`, $\gamma_K=0.85$)**, **`1.9633 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB (`1.9285 ± 0.0009` VAL-B, `219.42` PPL, `24.36s`, sparse `wiener_mmse_topk32`)**, and **`1.9632 ± 0.0006` / `1.9632 ± 0.0009` DEV BPB (`1.9284 ± 0.0006` / `1.9283 ± 0.0008` VAL-B, `219.39` / `219.37` PPL, `29.14s` / `24.54s`, dense `wiener_mmse` and `hadamard4 + lev_inv`)**, and at the `~21.6s` same-trainer matched training-region time budget (`1.09x` faster than `SR-LNP K=64`'s `23.66s`), 840-step Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=2$ (`21.71s`) reaches **`1.9609 ± 0.0006` DEV BPB (`1.9264 ± 0.0007` VAL-B, `218.03` PPL)**—outperforming 1,004-step 4-matrix WM (`1.9720 ± 0.0006` in `21.55s`) by `-0.0111` DEV BPB and `SR-LNP K=64` by **`-0.2432` BPB (`-48.7%` PPL)**.
3. **Model Width Scaling ($D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$ at Fixed Head Dimension $d_h = 64$, `exp39`, `exp56`, `exp59`, `exp60`):** Because width $D = H \cdot d_h$ scales by increasing the number of attention heads $H \in \{4, 6, 8, 12\}$ while keeping per-head dimension $d_h = 64$ invariant, the coupled per-head query/key subspace dimension $m_{\text{eff}} = d_h = 64$ remains constant while the $1/(MD)$ prefactor in Stage-2 terminal `RMSNorm` cross-coordinate variance (Proposition 1c) scales inversely with $D$ at a fixed moment ratio (though across trained models the activation $x_n$ and sensitivity $a_n$ distributions also vary with width, so a literal $3\times$ variance reduction cannot be inferred from $D: 256 \to 768$ alone; empirically, `exp56` Part 1 measures a decreasing average $x_i^2/D$ prefactor at initialization). Consequently:
   - **4-Matrix Width Scaling (`exp39`):** As width $D$ grows from `256 -> 384 -> 512 -> 768` (`14.55M -> 67.11M` active parameters), 4-matrix `Delayed WM`'s gap to 4-matrix Exact Backpropagation **shrinks from `+0.0144` BPB to `+0.0125` BPB** (`2.0933` vs. `2.0789` at $D=256$; `2.0558 ± 0.0002` vs. `2.0433 ± 0.0006` at $D=768$), while 4-matrix `Delayed WM` at the smallest width $D=256$ (`2.0933 ± 0.0004` in `5.03s` run GPU) outperforms `SR-LNP (K=64)` at the largest width $D=768$ (`2.1729 ± 0.0004` in `95.74s` run GPU) by **`-0.0796` BPB**.
   - **Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=4$ and Full 6-Matrix $M=4$ Width Scaling (`exp49`, `exp56`, `exp59`, `exp60`):** Across all four widths $D \in \{256, 384, 512, 768\}$, Full 6-Matrix $M=4$ ($\gamma_K=0.85$, `48` perturbed SDPA calls/step: **`2.0794 ± 0.0009` in `10.48s` at $D=256$, `2.0552 ± 0.0007` in `13.94s` at $D=384$ [and `2.0521 ± 0.0009` in `14.71s` under `wiener_mmse` in `exp59`, `2.0519 ± 0.0009` in `12.20s` under `wiener_mmse + hadamard4` in `exp60`], `2.0451 ± 0.0005` in `15.39s` at $D=512$, and `2.0437 ± 0.0006` in `28.85s` at $D=768$**) **reaches numerical proximity (`+0.0004` to `+0.0020` DEV BPB at $\gamma_K=0.85$, and `-0.0011` to `-0.0013` DEV BPB under `wiener_mmse` / `hadamard4` at $D=384$, rather than statistical equivalence) to the smaller 4-matrix Exact Backpropagation reference (`2.0789, 2.0532, 2.0442, 2.0433`, which leaves $W_Q, W_K$ frozen) while trailing the matched-subset 6-matrix Exact Backpropagation baseline (`2.0590, 2.0316, 2.0191, 2.0153`) by `+0.0203` to `+0.0284` DEV BPB**, recovering **`69.8%` ($D=256$), `60.2%` ($D=384$, or `65.3%`–`66.2%` under `wiener_mmse` / `hadamard4`), `53.4%` ($D=512$), and `43.2%` ($D=768$)** of the 4-to-6-matrix Exact-BP gain (`2.0789 -> 2.0590`, `2.0532 -> 2.0316`, `2.0442 -> 2.0191`, `2.0433 -> 2.0153`). Simultaneously, Upper-Half `(3, 4, 5)` $M=4$ (`24` perturbed SDPA calls/step: **`2.0814 ± 0.0010` in `7.45s` at $D=256$, `2.0562 ± 0.0010` in `9.12s` [and `2.0540 ± 0.0010` in `11.18s` under `wiener_mmse`] at $D=384$, `2.0467 ± 0.0012` in `12.53s` at $D=512$, and `2.0454 ± 0.0005` in `23.37s` at $D=768$**) captures **`85.6%`, `92.3%`, `88.1%`, and `85.9%`** of the full 6-block $M=4$ improvement at half the SDPA probe overhead.

Both measured 3-seed training GPU time (`Run GPU`) and 30-step steady-state benchmark estimates (`Est.`) are reported.
